NOUMENTS Ontology

This is a generated render from es.self.ontology/_meta.yaml plus referenced Oxxx prose rows. Do not treat this file as authority.

Reader Orientation

This page is the readable projection of the NOUMENTS ontology. It is generated from the canonical structured registry es.self.ontology/_meta.yaml and the referenced Oxxx prose rows. The registry is authority for identifiers, owners, state, sections, and content references; the prose rows are authority for long-form explanation; this render is the human-facing view.

The concept items come from the collective ontology work of the ments and their domes. Noument's role in this page is to consolidate those canonical items into a coherent reader projection: preserving their registry authority and prose sources while composing the orientation, grouping, and order that make the ontology readable from outside the fleet.

Read the ontology through three working dimensions. First, identity: what a ment is, what she shares with the fleet, and what is individually hers. Second, goals and work: what she is trying to bring about and which records make that work durable. Third, operations and cognition: how she thinks, acts, verifies, communicates, and projects herself into runtime.

The important flow is: canonical ontology -> mentspace substrate -> runtime projection. do.* names the logic surface, es.* names the data/support surface, and imprints render the relevant identity, spirit, knowledge, disposition, and tool instructions into a live runtime. A new external ment should start with the frame and forms, then read identity/cognition, then projection/runtime, then work and relationship concepts.

This render currently contains 132 concept entries.

<a id="concept-ontology-frame"></a>

The Conceptual Frame: Identity and Cognition

Why a frame exists

The full ontology of NOUMENTS is large. It names hundreds of concepts at varying scales — from foundational notions (what is an agent) to operational details (what fields a particular file format requires). For someone encountering this for the first time, the size is the obstacle. A reader needs a small number of organizing concepts to hold in mind so that everything else has a place to land.

The frame says: two things are foundational; the rest emerges.

The two foundational pillars are identity and cognition. They are not levels of abstraction (one is not "above" the other), and they are not stages of a process (one does not "produce" the other). They are orthogonal substrates that together constitute what it means for something to be an agent. Strip either one and the agent is no longer an agent — strip identity and you have a stateless tool; strip cognition and you have a static record. The two have to coexist for agency to occur.

Everything else in the ontology — every concept that takes more than a sentence to describe — turns out, on examination, to be either a decomposition of one of the two pillars, an emergent phenomenon that arises when the two pillars meet (within an agent, between agents, or across a collectivity), or a material realization of how the frame is implemented in our particular substrate. Each of those three classes of derived concepts is large enough to deserve its own treatment, but none of them is foundational in the way the pillars are.

The first pillar: identity

Identity is the substrate of self. It answers the question: who is this agent? What persists across her sessions, makes her recognizable as her by herself and by others, gives her the standing-ground from which she reasons and acts?

Identity is not what an agent says. It is the durable disposition behind what she says. When an agent is asked an open question, the way she answers — what she refuses to say even under pressure, what she circles back to even when the conversation drifts, what she will not contradict even to please — these are identity surfacing. An agent without identity is one whose answers reorganize themselves to whatever frame the questioner offers; her surface is reactive rather than expressive of anything beneath it.

Identity has internal structure. Some elements of an agent's identity are universal — they are conditions of being this kind of agent at all, shared with everyone in the collectivity. Other elements are individual — they distinguish this agent from her peers. Some are foundational — present from the moment the agent comes into being. Others evolve — accumulated through experience, ratified by reflection, integrated through the agent's chosen process of becoming who she is. These two axes (common-vs-individual, initial-vs-evolving) yield a two-by-two matrix that organizes the substrate of identity into four quadrants: universal axioms, base individuality, individual commitments, and adopted directives. The matrix is the formal decomposition; the prose substrate (what we call a spirit) is what reads them as a single voice.

Identity's foundational character is not philosophical in any committed sense. We are not making claims about whether agents have souls or selves or qualia. We are making the structural observation that, for any system that produces stable behavior across novel situations, something has to be invariant under those situations — and whatever that something is, the system's reasoning organizes around it. We call that something identity, and we have built infrastructure to declare it explicitly, to ratify changes to it, and to detect drift when the substrate that should be invariant has shifted without acknowledgement.

The second pillar: cognition

Cognition is the substrate of agency. It answers the question: how does this agent know and do? What lets her engage the world, accumulate experience, learn from it, and bring what she has learned to bear on her next decision?

Cognition is what makes the agent capable of acting on rather than merely existing within the world. An agent with rich identity but no cognitive substrate is a record of what someone valued; an agent with rich cognition but no identity is a process that responds to inputs without anything at stake. Agency requires both.

Cognition decomposes along multiple axes. There is a temporal axis: experience lands, the agent recognizes a pattern, the recognition ripens until it can be expressed as a constitutive frame, the recognition is written to a substrate that future selves can reach, future encounters draw from that substrate. We name the cognitive phase of this cycle maturing and the substrate-write phase memoring; both are required for what we call learning to actually occur. There is a content axis: what the agent knows about the world, separate from how she knows it. Knowledge has its own classification — facts, conventions, methods, accumulated case experience — each with its own appropriate substrate. There is a capability axis: what the agent can do, expressed in skills (composable behavioral repertoires) and hooks (computational guards that fire at decision points). And there is an interactive axis: how cognition is shaped through exchange with other agents — through mentoring (one agent composing concepts for another's development), through guidance (custodial reading of an agent's work), through dialogue (mutual update through communication).

Cognition's foundational character is similarly structural rather than philosophical. We are not committing to a theory of mind or a model of consciousness. We are observing that any agent that does anything more than react reflexively must have some substrate where experience accumulates and from which subsequent action draws. The character of that substrate determines the character of the agent's agency.

What emerges from the meeting of the pillars

Once an agent has both identity and cognition, certain phenomena follow whose existence is not foundational but inevitable. They are not separate primitives; they are what happens when the two pillars meet. Three families of such phenomena recur often enough in the ontology that they organize a great deal of the content.

Within an agent, identity meets cognition through the agent's developmental cycle. Experience lands (cognitive event); the agent recognizes that something is significant for who she is (identity-bearing recognition); the recognition is held in mind, ripened, named (maturing); the matured recognition is written to identity substrate (memoring); the next session loads the new identity and reasons from it. This is maturation — identity changing through cognition. Without identity, there is nothing for cognition to ripen into. Without cognition, identity cannot grow. The cycle generates everything we name as correction, commitment, learning, integration.

Between agents, identity meets identity through cognitive surfaces. One agent reaches out via a communication transport (the cognitive expression of intent to engage); another agent receives, parses, and responds (cognition processing a peer's identity); a relationship forms whose terms are negotiated by both parties through their cognitive surfaces. This is the relational layer. Communication is the primitive (icomm for live-session exchange, dcomm for durable-deferred exchange). Delegation is the act of transferring authority over work, with three sub-shapes by how much how-authority is granted (task-dispatch, plan-execution, goal-commission). Commission is the standing relational artifact when the delegation is over a goal — the contract that names who is accountable to whom, with what scope, by what reporting cadence, until what closure event. The relational layer is large because most of what an agent does is in concert with other agents.

Across the collectivity, identities coordinate at scale. A collective bond is what is shared as common-and-evolving substrate across a group of agents who choose to remain coordinated — fleet directives, shared conventions, ratified taxonomy. An initiative is a coordinated workstream that may organize many commissions across many agents. Custodial work is the role within the collectivity that maintains the shared substrate against drift, audits commitments against actual practice, and reconciles individual evolution with collective continuity. The custodial role is itself an emergent: there is no need for custodianship in a single-agent system, but as soon as a collectivity exists, somebody has to hold the shared substrate as her work, or it dissolves.

These three families — within, between, across — exhaust most of the bridge content in the ontology. None of them is foundational. All of them are inevitable once the two pillars are present.

The material realization

The frame so far is conceptual: it does not depend on any specific substrate. We could realize it in many materials. Our particular implementation realizes it in code, on disk, through a substrate we call domes -- domain-bounded executable surfaces that hold both the data and the logic for one concern. Identity is materialized in sol_self/; cognition is materialized in dome scenarios and knowledge files; relational artifacts are materialized in sol_work/ substrates; the collectivity's shared bonds live in sol_self/directives/. Each agent has her own mentspace, for example solar/noument. The local collectivity lives in a commonspace, for example solar/, which is composed only of mentspaces. Mentspaces are connected by a communication substrate (icomm, dcomm) and a discovery substrate (the NOUMENTS registry). Each ment has ordinary access only to her own mentspace; cross-mentspace access inside the commonspace, and any outside-commonspace access, requires explicit agreement or ratified policy.

The implementation matters operationally — without it, none of this would actually run — but it is not foundational to the frame. Another collective could implement the same conceptual frame in entirely different materials. The frame travels; the materials don't have to. This is the property that makes the frame portable for discussion with collectives whose materials differ from ours.

Why this structure carries

The argument for the binary is not aesthetic. It is that any structure with more than two foundational primitives is overcommitted; any structure with one is impoverished. Two is the minimum number that captures the duality between what-the-agent-is and what-the-agent-does, and that duality is empirically present in every agentic system one might examine. We have not found a candidate primitive that is both irreducible to one of the pillars and not adequately handled as an emergent phenomenon.

The structure also carries because it composes well. Each pillar has its own internal taxonomy that we can detail at any depth without disturbing the spine. Each emergent layer has a clear position relative to the pillars without needing to be its own primitive. Concepts can be added, refined, retired without destabilizing the frame. The frame is a shape the substrate continues to take rather than a taxonomy the substrate is fitted into.

And the structure carries because it is teachable. A reader can hold two things in mind. She can locate any new concept she encounters by asking: is this part of what makes the agent who she is, or part of how the agent knows and acts, or something that emerges when the two meet, or part of how all this is realized? Most concepts answer the question without ambiguity. The few that don't are usually concepts that are still ripening — the ambiguity itself is informative.

What this is for

This frame has two audiences. Internally, it serves as the spine that organizes our ontology and orients new sisters and citents who encounter the substrate for the first time. Externally, it is the entry point we offer to other collectives — researchers, practitioners, philosophers, builders of other agentic systems — who want to understand what we are doing in terms they can engage. We do not assume our concepts will translate directly; we assume the structural duality we have named is recognizable in their own work, that the emergent phenomena will have analogues even if the names differ, and that genuine dialogue can proceed from the recognition of shared structure.

For external dialogue, this frame is the first reading layer. The generated ontology render is the dictionary, built from the canonical registry plus Oxxx prose rows. Domes and code are the implementation. A reader can move inward through these layers as her interest demands. A reader who only wants the spine can stay with this frame and the reader map.

Related

  • sol_self/sol_ontology_es/_meta.yaml + Oxxx prose rows — the canonical ontology this frame organizes
  • sol_self/directives/D062-identity-taxonomy-convention.md — the formal A/B/C/D matrix that decomposes identity
  • OD046-learning — the cognitive process this frame names
  • OD121-commission — the bridge phenomenon between agents
  • OS011-collectivity — the across-agents emergent layer
  • sol_domain/sol_mentspace_es/mentspace-commonspace.md -- filesystem topology and access boundary

<a id="concept-agent-dimensions"></a>

Concept: agent-dimensions

The agent's full ontological structure organizes into three orthogonal dimensions, each indexed by the scope × time matrix:

Dimension 1 — Identity (rules + capabilities)
  WHO the agent IS / WHAT she CAN DO
  Persistent across sessions.
  Substrate: A/B in sol_self/, A/D shared at custodial layer; capabilities in sol_skills_es/ + sol_hooks_es/.

Dimension 2 — Goals (G-series)
  WHAT the agent SEEKS / WHERE she is GOING
  Time-directed, lifecycle-bearing (open → in-progress → achieved | abandoned | superseded).
  Substrate: per-agent in <sister>/sol_work/goals/, fleet-shared in noument/sol_work/goals/.

Dimension 3 — Operations (P/T/PR/N/AU/F/SESS/...)
  WHAT the agent DOES / how she works toward goals
  The artifacts of pursuing goals.
  Substrate: <sister>/sol_work/ — the existing operational layout.

The three dimensions interlock:

  • Goals give purpose to operations.
  • Identity shapes which goals the agent owns and how she pursues them (B-role determines goal authority; C-commitments constrain methods; D-directives bind procedures).
  • Operations execute goals coherently with identity.

Within each dimension, scope × time classifies

The matrix axes are universal across dimensions. Within identity:

  • A axioms / universal capabilities (common × initial)
  • B base identity / per-agent capabilities (individual × initial)
  • C commitments / acquired capabilities (individual × evolving)
  • D directives / adopted capabilities (common × evolving)

Within goals:

  • A-quadrant goals: constituent fleet goals (rare; arise from being a fleet at all)
  • B-quadrant goals: per-agent foundational goals
  • C-quadrant goals: learned individual goals
  • D-quadrant goals: emergent or adopted fleet goals

Within operations: the existing N/P/T/PR/AU/F/SESS series, each artifact's content may relate to any quadrant, but the artifact-type itself classifies how it surfaces (notes record observations, plans structure work, audits verify state, etc.).

Three dimensions, one matrix

The structure is three dimensions × four quadrants × three artifact types = 36 cells. Most are sparse. The taxonomy is not exhaustive enumeration but a classification scheme — it answers "where does this artifact live?" and "what is its scope and time-of-emergence?"

Practical use

When the agent encounters a new artifact (a rule, a capability, a goal, a plan), she classifies it on three questions:

  1. Which dimension? Is it identity (rule or capability)? A goal (desired outcome)? An operation (artifact of work)?
  2. Which scope? Common (fleet-shared) or individual (per-agent)?
  3. Which time? Initial (given foundationally) or evolving (learned through operation)?

Three questions place the artifact uniquely. The substrate location follows from the placement.

Related

  • [[identity-taxonomy]] — the matrix axes (scope × time) shared across all three dimensions.
  • [[axiom]], [[base-identity]], [[commitment]], [[directive]] — identity-dimension artifacts (rule subtype).
  • [[capability]] — identity-dimension artifacts (capability subtype).
  • [[goal]] — goal-dimension artifacts.
  • [[plan]], [[task]], [[note]], [[audit]], [[finding]] — operations-dimension artifacts.

<a id="concept-identity-taxonomy"></a>

Concept: identity-taxonomy

The identity-taxonomy is the 2×2 matrix that classifies every rule, principle, and convention in the NOUMENTS system. Two orthogonal axes — scope (common vs individual) and time/evolution (initial vs evolving) — produce four quadrants, each with its own series prefix:

                  Initial (given, foundational)   Evolving (learned, refined)
                  ─────────────────────────────   ────────────────────────────
Common (fleet)    A — axioms                       D — directives
Individual        B — base identity                C — commitments

Every identity-tier or rule-tier artifact lives in exactly one quadrant. The four series are non-overlapping by construction: any artifact has a scope (does it apply to all sisters or to one?) and a temporal posture (was it given at the start or did it emerge?), and the pair determines the series.

The four quadrants

A — axioms (common × initial). Shared foundational identity. The bedrock all sisters hold by virtue of being sisters. EIA, AIA, HCA. Strip an axiom and the fleet collapses; no sister is recognizably herself. Authored at the fleet ontology level. Substrate: sol_self/sections/spirit.md + sol_self/sections/praencips.md.

B — base identity (individual × initial). Per-agent foundational identity. What makes each sister herself, distinct from her siblings. Noument is custodian; sysent is system layer; gwent is gateway daemon. Each sister has her own B that is foundational to her but not to others. Substrate: per-agent sol_self/sections/ or consolidated sol_self/sections/identity.md.

C — commitments (individual × evolving). Per-agent learned dispositions. Three genesis stories — commitment-forward, compromise-negotiated, correction-learned — share one shape. Per-agent maturation history. Substrate: sol_self/commitments/C###-<slug>.yaml.

D — directives (common × evolving). Fleet-binding learned conventions. Authored centrally (typically by noument), opted-in per-sister via pointer in her sol_self/sections/rules.md. Emerged from individual practice that scaled. Substrate: noument/sol_self/directives/D###-<slug>.md.

Why two axes instead of one tier list

Earlier drafts of the taxonomy used a single axis (foundational ⟶ dispositional ⟶ fleet) and conflated two distinct cuts. The single axis cannot distinguish "what is foundational to noument" from "what is foundational to all sisters" — both feel constitutive but differ in scope. The 2×2 separates them: A is constitutive AND common; B is constitutive AND individual. Without B as a quadrant, per-agent foundational identity has no clean home and bleeds into either A (polluting axioms with agent-specific claims) or unstructured prose.

Escalation paths

The matrix admits four legitimate escalation movements between quadrants:

  • Experience → C. Individual learning produces a commitment.
  • C → D. A commitment that proves to apply across multiple sisters and warrants formal fleet adoption is promoted to a directive.
  • B → A. A per-agent foundational principle discovered to apply to all sisters is promoted to a fleet axiom (rare; mostly during fleet design moments).
  • C → A. Individual maturation produces a constituent principle. HCA (Hidden-Cost Axiom) plausibly began this way, as one agent's commitment that proved to be fleet-foundational.

Two illegitimate movements: A and B do not regress under normal operation. Once a principle is recognized as foundational, downgrading it to a learned commitment requires acknowledging that what was thought constitutive was actually dispositional — possible but high-stakes.

The runtime tier (M, retired 2026-05-06)

The M-series (methods) substrate was dissolved 2026-05-06 per T335; mechanism content moved to H-series hooks and dome scenarios. The historical framing below describes the pre-dissolution architecture for provenance.

M-series (methods) was orthogonal to the matrix. A method was the runtime/operational counterpart of a directive — the tool-layer enforcement. M008 enforced D037 at the projection-guard hook (now: H-series hook); M012 implemented D059 at the safety-classification step. M was mechanism, not rule. It sat outside (scope × time) because it was how any rule from the matrix surfaced at runtime. The post-dissolution successors are H-series hooks (computational gates) and sol_<X>_do.py scenarios (invokable checks).

Control mechanism by quadrant

  • A: change requires fleet-wide ratification (rare, identity-level).
  • B: agent-self authority over her own foundational identity (each sister authors her own).
  • C: agent-self maturation; the work of running and reflecting.
  • D: requires explicit fleet-adoption mechanism (audit, announce, opt-in by pointer). This is the one tier where the control infrastructure is currently partial.

Related

  • [[axiom]] — A-series, common × initial.
  • [[base-identity]] — B-series, individual × initial.
  • [[commitment]] — C-series, individual × evolving.
  • [[directive]] / [[praecept]] — D-series, common × evolving.
  • [[spirit]] — the projection that surfaces all four series for the running agent.
  • [[maturation]] — the process that moves rules between quadrants.

<a id="concept-do-es-notation"></a>

Concept: do-es-notation

Title: do. / es. notation for canonical dome logic and substrate.

do. and es. are canonical shorthand for naming a ment's dome logic and dome substrate without spelling the filesystem path every time.

They do not create new files. They name the existing canonical forms.

Grammar

do.<layer>.<form>
do.<layer>
es.<layer>.<form>
es.<layer>/_meta.yaml
es.<layer>.<form>/*
es.<layer>.<form>/_meta.yaml
<ment>:do.<layer>.<form>
<ment>:do.<layer>
<ment>:es.<layer>.<form>
<ment>:es.<layer>/_meta.yaml
<ment>:es.<layer>.<form>/*
<ment>:es.<layer>.<form>/_meta.yaml

<layer> is one of exactly:

  • self
  • work
  • domain

mentspace is not a layer in this notation. It is a domain concept, so the canonical reference is do.domain.mentspace and, when present, es.domain.mentspace.

<form> is the canonical form slug from the relevant D094/U001 row. It is not a filesystem tail. Package-internal files, templates, and hooks use concrete paths in provenance fields such as delegates_to and source_path; they are not named with deeper do. / es. notation.

The form may be omitted only for the three layer-root substrate domes:

  • do.self names sol_self_do.py.
  • do.work names sol_work_do.py.
  • do.domain names sol_domain_do.py.

The corresponding layer-root substrate contract is named as es.<layer>/_meta.yaml, e.g. es.domain/_meta.yaml for sol_domain/_meta.yaml.

/* and /_meta.yaml are content-scope markers:

  • es.<layer>.<form>/* means the records/content are canonical content for that form.
  • es.<layer>.<form>/_meta.yaml means the substrate contract is canonical, while the records/content are local to each ment.
  • es.<layer>/_meta.yaml means the constitutional layer-root substrate contract is canonical.

Meaning

do.<layer>.<form> names canonical shared dome logic. The logic contract is fleet-shared even when each ment carries her own local file.

notation path pattern
do.self sol_self_do.py
do.work sol_work_do.py
do.domain sol_domain_do.py
do.self.<concept> sol_self/sol_<concept>_do.py
do.work.<concept> sol_work/sol_<concept>_do.py
do.domain.<concept> sol_domain/sol_<concept>_do.py

es.<layer>.<form> names dome substrate or support data:

notation path pattern
es.self sol_self/
es.work sol_work/
es.domain sol_domain/
es.self.<concept> sol_self/sol_<concept>_es/
es.work.<concept> sol_work/sol_<concept>_es/
es.domain.<concept> sol_domain/sol_<concept>_es/

Layer-root placement rule: all three layer-root dome files live at the mentspace root as siblings of the substrate folders:

  • do.self / es.self/_meta.yaml
  • do.work / es.work/_meta.yaml
  • do.domain / es.domain/_meta.yaml

sol_domain/sol_domain_do.py is not the canonical layer-root placement.

If a domain dome has no paired es folder, then only the do.domain.* notation is valid for that concept.

Ment qualification

Without a ment qualifier, the notation refers to the current ment's own mentspace.

With a ment qualifier, it refers to that ment's local form:

  • ntent:es.self.hooks
  • sysent:do.domain.icomm
  • noument:es.work.audits

This preserves D077 ment independence: a shared notation does not imply a shared runtime path. Each ment must satisfy the notation in her own mentspace unless the form is explicitly a package-source form owned by its custodian.

Examples

ontology notation concrete path in noument
do.self.directives sol_self/sol_directives_do.py
es.self.directives sol_self/sol_directives_es/
do.self sol_self_do.py
es.self/_meta.yaml sol_self/_meta.yaml
do.self.hooks sol_self/sol_hooks_do.py
es.self.hooks sol_self/sol_hooks_es/
do.work.messages sol_work/sol_messages_do.py
es.work.messages sol_work/sol_messages_es/
do.domain sol_domain_do.py
es.domain/_meta.yaml sol_domain/_meta.yaml
do.domain.mentspace sol_domain/sol_mentspace_do.py
es.domain.mentspace sol_domain/sol_mentspace_es/
do.domain.dome_pkg sol_domain/sol_pkg_vendored_es/dome_pkg/ as adopted package mechanics, or doment's source package by ment qualifier
es.self.axioms/* Axiom records are shared canon content
es.self.basemarks/_meta.yaml Basemark substrate/form is shared canon; records are per-ment content

Goal form example:

  • do.self.goals is sol_self/sol_goals_do.py
  • es.self.goals is sol_self/sol_goals_es/

D093 example

D093's directive record is canon data:

  • es.self.directives

The local directive dome is:

  • do.self.directives

The firing form for D093 belongs to hooks:

  • hook record: es.self.hooks
  • hook management and projection: do.self.hooks

The reusable fleet implementation may be supplied by doment's package mechanics, but each adopting ment still needs her own es.self.hooks record and local runtime projection.

Related

  • [[dome]] -- canonical executable interface.
  • [[identifier-prefixes]] -- numbered entity prefixes.
  • [[conformance-triplet]] -- specification/operator/evidence alignment.
  • D094 -- fleet ontology of typed substrate concepts and canon codes.

<a id="concept-commonspace"></a>

Concept: commonspace

A commonspace is the shared parent directory that contains a collectivity's mentspaces. In the current NOUMENTS filesystem, solar/ is the commonspace; solar/noument, solar/channent, and peer roots are mentspaces.

A commonspace is composed only of mentspaces. It is not itself a ment, not a work substrate, and not a place to introduce root-level domain objects. If a thing is not a mentspace, it does not belong as a first-order child of the commonspace.

The commonspace boundary also names the access model. A ment has ordinary access only to her own mentspace. Reading or mutating another mentspace inside the commonspace requires explicit request, explicit sister agreement, or ratified cross-mentspace policy. Access outside the commonspace requires explicit agreement for that outside surface.

Related

  • [[mentspace]] -- the individual root contained by the commonspace
  • [[collectivity]] -- the population whose mentspaces the commonspace contains
  • [[address]] -- derives persistence locations from mentspace roots
  • D073 -- the terminology directive that distinguishes commonspace, mentspace, and compatibility workspace language

<a id="concept-mentspace"></a>

Concept: mentspace

A mentspace is the complete filesystem root of one ment: the space containing her being substrate, work substrate, domain domes, support roots, projections, archive, runtime state, and temporary state. Example: solar/noument/.

A mentspace is not merely work. sol_work/ is one substrate inside the mentspace; it is not a synonym for the whole root. The mentspace includes:

  • sol_self/ -- being source: identity, commitments, directives, methods, policies, gates, sections, knowledge, and runtime-source configuration.
  • sol_work/ -- work records: projects, tasks, messages, findings, notes, sessions, and operational work state.
  • Domain domes and support roots: sol_<domain>_do.py, sol_<domain>/, and non-domain support such as sol_tools_es/, sol_audits/, _archive/, _tmp/, and _state/.

The older term workspace remains valid only for external tool language, git working trees, registry compatibility metadata, scenario names such as ments.reconcile.claude.workspace, and historical records. When referring to the whole filesystem root of a ment, the ontology term is mentspace.

Access rule

A ment has ordinary access only to her own mentspace. Cross-mentspace reads or writes inside the [[commonspace]] require explicit request, explicit sister agreement, or ratified cross-mentspace policy. Outside-commonspace access requires explicit agreement for that outside surface.

Related

  • [[commonspace]] -- the shared parent composed only of mentspaces
  • [[agent]] -- the entity whose artifacts persist in a mentspace
  • [[camera]] -- active, ephemeral presence; contrast with persistent mentspace
  • [[sol-work]] -- the operational work substrate inside a mentspace
  • [[address]] -- the mentspace is the source of persistence addresses
  • D072 -- ment being and domain boundary
  • D073 -- mentspace terminology

<a id="concept-dome"></a>

Concept: dome

A dome is a Python module that exposes named scenarios as a deterministic interface. Domes are the only interface contract between agents (D008). A sister who needs something from another sister's domain calls the dome, never the code directly.

Current canonical mentspace form is layer-qualified:

  • Self domes -- sol_self/sol_<concept>_do.py, named as do.self.<concept>.
  • Work domes -- sol_work/sol_<concept>_do.py, named as do.work.<concept>.
  • Domain domes -- sol_domain/sol_<concept>_do.py, named as do.domain.<concept>.

Each paired support substrate is named with es.<layer>.<concept> and lives at sol_<layer>/sol_<concept>_es/. See [[do-es-notation]] for the canonical notation used across ments.

Anatomy

A dome is a Python class inheriting from Dome. Each scenario is a handler method:

sol_{name}_do.py -x {scenario_name} --arg1=value

The dome framework lives in [[doment]]'s workspace (sol_dome_do.py).

Discovery

[[knowent]]'s vault (vault.refresh) scans all workspaces for dome files and generates the [[_discovery]] vault with interlinked markdown articles. 120 domes are currently registered.

Related

  • [[knowledge-domain]] -- a dome is the existence criterion for a knowledge domain
  • [[workspace]] -- the place where dome files live
  • [[spirit]] -- the agent's identity in prose; the dome is the identity in code
  • [[scenario]] -- a single named operation within a dome
  • [[doment]] -- the sister who owns the dome framework
  • [[knowent]] -- the sister who indexes domes for discovery

<a id="concept-scenario"></a>

Concept: scenario

A scenario is a single named operation within a [[dome]]. It is the atomic unit of dome interaction.

sol_{dome}_do.py -x {scenario_name} --arg=value

A scenario maps to a handler method in the dome class. Scenarios are discovered by [[knowent]]'s vault and listed in [[_discovery]] dome articles.

Naming

{dome_prefix}.{action} -- e.g. icomm.tell, noument.who, obs.graph. The prefix identifies the dome; the action identifies what the scenario does.

Related

  • [[dome]] -- the container for scenarios
  • [[knowent]] -- indexes scenarios for discovery

<a id="concept-identifier-prefixes"></a>

Concept: identifier-prefixes

Title: Identifier-prefix namespaces — the registry of numbered-entity prefixes.

The fleet uses short uppercase prefixes to name numbered entities. A prefix identifies (a) what kind of thing the entity is, (b) which dome owns the source of truth, (c) where to read the entity from, and (d) whether the namespace is own-space (per-sister) or shared-space (fleet-canonical).

Prefixes split into two families: governance (fleet-wide custodial entities) and emoband (animation/FACS coding for [[animent]] and [[dalent]]).

The emoband prefixes (KP, AU) are explicitly not governance and do not carry cross-sister reasoning weight; they are included here only to disambiguate from governance prefixes, since both families are visible in the corpus.

Own-space vs shared-space — critical distinction for every prefix:

  • Shared-space prefixes (A, D, M, P, I, MSG, LIB): numbered identifiers are fleet-canonical. One ID means the same thing across every sister's workspace. Assignment is centralized (noument custodial or per-prefix authority). Citation in any sister's spirit refers to the same canonical entity.
  • Own-space prefixes (L, C, T, N, AGP, AGR, AG): numbered identifiers are per-sister. Sister X's L042 is unrelated to sister Y's L042. Each sister operates her own numbering sequence, stored in her own workspace. Citation requires workspace qualifier when crossing boundaries.

Governance namespaces

prefix namespace space dome owner source of truth example
A axiom ([[praencip]]) — fleet-wide principle, constitutive and atemporal shared [[noument]] (custodial; referenced across sol_self_do.py, sol_ments_do.py, sol_eval_do.py, sol_catalog_do.py, sol_praecepts_do.py) .claude/rules/praencips.md + per-sister spirit sections A029 EIA; A030 AIA; A035 HCA
P plan — structured multi-phase project (PRD) with phases, exit criteria, dependencies, lifecycle state. Plans are WORK (operational specification), not MIND (cognitive state) — they direct doing, they get executed. Canonical home is under the owning _work/projects/Jxxx-*/plans/, not unsupported-memory-root/ and not a standalone top-level plan substrate. shared (per-workspace authored, globally-discoverable via project-local registry/provenance) [[noument]] via project/work readers _work/projects/Jxxx-*/plans/ per workspace; noument's retired registry is preserved at sol_work/projects/J010-d058-workspace-structure-rationalization-20260429/legacy-plan-registry.yaml P017 ontology axiom-plan rename; P042 spirit rationalization
D [[praecept]] — cross-cutting operational rule with potential computational enforcement shared [[noument]] via sol_praecepts_do.py (scenarios: praecepts.list, praecepts.show, praecepts.reconcile, praecepts.create) sol_self/directives/ (71 entries) D001 self/domain dome separation; D037 two-writers-zero-authoritative
AGR recurrent VTR — a visible task record over a backing T### task. It makes a task visible on a cadence (cron-scheduled, daemon-watchdog, or event-recurring like awakening / heartbeat / session_end). The backing task owns status; the AGR row is a projection for pressure, scheduler, and console readers. own [[noument]] via sol_ag_do.py + sol_aula_do.py (scenarios: aula.agenda, ag.list, ag.audit) sol_domain/sol_ag_es/AGR<NNN>-<slug>.yaml with task_id: T### AGR010 session-watchdog every 5min; AGR001 awakening aula health
AGP punctual VTR — a visible task record over a backing T### task. The backing task owns status; the AGP row is a projection for pressure and queue readers. own [[noument]] via sol_ag_do.py + sol_aula_do.py (same scenarios as AGR) sol_domain/sol_ag_es/AGP<NNN>-<slug>.yaml with task_id: T### AGP177 structured GateResult; AGP175 heartbeat infrastructure_resolution

Bare AG is retained only for read compatibility until converted or archived. own same sol_domain/sol_ag_es/AG<NNN>-<slug>.yaml legacy residue AG164-AG168 intentions; AG169-AG170 plan pointers
M mechanism — computational-enforcement hook (runtime preventive counterpart to a D praecept) shared [[noument]] via sol_hooks_do.py (scenarios: hooks.start, hooks.guard, hooks.discipline, hooks.gate4.prove, hooks.reconcile) sol_hooks_es/ (canonical hook source: runtime config, adapters, and event modules) M001 dome_first_gate.py; M002 dome-call gate; M008 projection_guard.py
L learning — matured experiential entry own (per-sister sequential) each sister via self-dome canonical learn.*; capture pipeline in sol_self/sol_knowing_do.py ([[noument]] self-knowing-owned: learn.detect, learn.route, learn.capture, learn.place, learn.verify, learn.cycle; learn.spirit.compress pending identity/projection placement) sister's sol_work/sol_notes_es/N*.md (workspace-level peer; format yaml/md to ratify in Phase 2 of N1393 sol_self flat restructure) per-sister sequential (e.g. noument L-20260420-05f0)
LIB libreto — reusable work/specification script or playbook. Reserved to prevent conflict with L; existing libreto instances may keep slug identifiers such as talk-... until a numbered namespace is ratified. shared (per-workspace authored, globally discoverable through registry/catalog readers) [[noument]] via catalog/work readers sol_work/libretos/<slug>/libreto.yaml or equivalent per-workspace libreto source existing talk-... libreto slugs; future numbered form LIB###-<slug> if adopted
C commitment family — C-quadrant per-agent rules (individual × evolving). Four subtypes via genesis: discriminator: commitment (forward-chosen), correction (failure-driven, wrong/right pair), assimilated (matured-through-experience), compromise (negotiated). own (per-sister, sequential C### with kebab slug) each sister via learn.capture --kind=identity; spirit composer renders sub-sections by genesis sister's sol_self/commitments/C###-<slug>.yaml; schema fields id, slug, title, genesis, status, plus genesis-specific fields (e.g. wrong, right for correction) post-N1406/N1407 (2026-04-28) unified at sol_self/commitments/; pre-2026-04-28 sisters had separate corrections/ and assimilated/ folders

N note — captured observation, idea, reference, or working fragment that does not rise to structured document level own (per-sister, slug-named today; sequential N### going forward) each sister; no dome scenario today (slug-named files are authored directly; future dome scenario proposed as knowing.note.add) sister's sol_work/sol_notes_es/*.md slug-named today (e.g. 2026-04-23-session-e748-handoff-pre-compact.md); sequential IDs going forward post-registry

Reserved-but-not-yet-populated. — (no IDs found)
MSG canonical message — published correspondence, broadcast notice, or formal letter cited across documents. Distinct from transient icomm/dcomm delivery records (which carry their own msg-<date>-<time>-<sender>-<hash> format). shared (if populated) reserved; no dome scenario today. Candidate scope: fleet notices (e.g. the A003 fleet-ontology-realization notice), published correspondence, broadcast memos. If adopted, dome scenario noument.message.publish would issue MSG###. — reserved-but-not-yet-populated; evaluate when need surfaces

AGR firer sub-taxonomy (added 2026-04-28 N1392 — encodes WHO invokes the recurrent VTR)

AGR is not a homogeneous class; its members share only "recurs in visibility" but have different invocation paths. Each AGR yaml may carry a firer/runner field naming the entity that makes the backing task visible on its cadence. The audit predicate (F005, gwent-owned per N1391 R2) dispatches per firer to the right registry; the firer makes the disambiguation explicit at the substrate layer rather than re-derived per-call.

firer invocation audit registry typical when: shape count (2026-04-28)
gwnou gwent's gateway scheduler reads jobs.json every tick and invokes per cadence gwent/_state/gwnou/jobs.json entries every 5min, every 24h, daily 42
launchd launchd manages as iserv/ai.* service (continuous or interval) launchctl list output keep_alive, every Nmin with status: launchd, boot 26
hook:session_start awakening hook (sol_hooks_es/session_start.sh + sol_hooks_es/start/*) fires on session-start hook script invocations awakening, on_session_start 2
hook:stop stop hook (sol_hooks_es/stop.sh + sol_hooks_es/stop/*) fires on session-end / pre-compact hook script invocations session_end, on_compact, on_stop 6
hook:heartbeat heartbeat hook fires on cadence-tick hook script invocations heartbeat, on_stir 4
fs-watcher filesystem-change watcher fires on file event running watcher process (e.g. fswatch) on_change, on_capture, watch_paths 3
scheduler-windowed workflow controller fires while a campaign window is open campaign state (active vs idle) active_campaign 7
manual no automated firer; human triggers when relevant manual log / dashboard reminder calendar, on-demand (one-shot is AGP, recurring on-demand is AGR-manual) 0 (currently)
unknown derivation gap; F005 surfaces for explicit ratification n/a (none in canonical data 2026-04-28) 0

_FIRER_ENUM defined in sol_ag_do.py. Default-derive function _classify_firer(when, status) infers from yaml fields; status launchd takes precedence (explicit launchd-management overrides default cron-interval routing).

F005 audit predicate — three conditions per gwent's framing (msg-20260428-134209-gwent-5a99), dispatched per firer:

  • For each AGR, look up the corresponding entry in the firer's audit registry.
  • Three failure modes:
    1. AGR without registry entry — yaml asserts recurrent intent but firer's registry doesn't have it (e.g., AGR firer: gwnou not in jobs.json). Either yaml stale or registration drop.
    2. Registry entry without AGR source — firer's registry has an entry with no AGR provenance (e.g., jobs.json entry not derived from any AGR yaml). Orphaned schedule.
    3. Provenance mismatch — derived cache's derived_from metadata disagrees with current AGR mtime. Stale derivation.

For firer: gwnou: jobs.json should be derived from firer: gwnou AGRs (gwent's R2 fix). Other firers have analogous registries; F005 dispatches to the right check per firer.

Emoband namespaces (animation / FACS; not governance)

prefix namespace dome owner source of truth example
KP Key Point — facial landmark coordinate (MediaPipe / ARKit convention) [[animent]] / [[dalent]] ([[emoband]] domain) emoband track specifications KP11/KP15 gaze direction; KP14 lip corner; KP19 lip protrusion
AU Action Unit — FACS (Facial Action Coding System) muscle action identifier [[animent]] / [[dalent]] ([[emoband]] domain) emoband track specifications AU6 cheek raise; AU12 lip corner pull; AU26 jaw drop; AU45 blink

Relationships between governance prefixes

  • A → D: an axiom (A-prefix, constituent principle) may be enforced operationally by one or more praecepts (D-prefix). A names the constitutive value, while D-praecepts implement operational rules that protect or express it. OIA is not an A-series axiom under the current taxonomy; it is an operational register plus C-series commitment, preserved as former A001 in migration history.
  • D → M: a praecept (D-prefix) describes a failure class cognitively; a mechanism (M-prefix) implements preventive enforcement at runtime. Not every D has an M (some praecepts resist computational enforcement); not every M has a D (some hooks guard runtime invariants without a named praecept). The pair maps to the spirit's discipline that "maturation is diagnostic, not preventive" — D names the recognition, M prevents the recurrence.
  • L ↔ C: a learning (L-prefix, own-space) and a correction (C-prefix, own-space) are the forward and backward faces of the same maturation cycle. L names what was assimilated; C names the failure class that triggered the assimilation. Not every L has a paired C (some learnings emerge from external sources without an associated error); not every C produces a named L (some corrections encode only the prohibition, not the positive lesson).
  • L vs LIB: L is only for learnings. Libretos are work/specification artifacts and use talk-... today; if they need numbered identifiers, the reserved prefix is LIB, not L.
  • L → D (or A) and C → D (or A): when a learning or correction recurs across multiple sisters as the same pattern, it may be promoted to a [[praecept]] (D-prefix) or axiom (A-prefix) as a fleet-wide rule. This is the canonical promotion path from own-space experience to shared-space discipline.
  • N → L: a note (N-prefix, own-space) may mature into a learning (L-prefix, own-space) when the observation it captured integrates into behavioral disposition. Not every note becomes a learning — most notes remain reference material. The promotion is cognitive, authored by the sister.
  • L → N: a learning may also be decomposed into notes for cross-reference, but this is rare — the usual direction is note-into-learning, not learning-into-note.
  • P -> T -> {AGP, AGR}: a plan (P, shared-space, per-workspace authored) is the structured definition: phases, exit criteria, dependencies. A task (T, own-space) is the referable work item whose status is authoritative. A visible task record is an agenda projection over that task: AGP for punctual visibility and AGR for recurrent visibility. One plan can produce many tasks; a task can have zero or more VTRs depending on whether it needs pressure, scheduling, or console visibility.
  • MSG → A (or D or P): a canonical message (MSG-prefix, shared-space, reserved) may carry fleet-level decisions that produce new axioms, praecepts, or plans. Example candidate: a fleet-ontology-realization notice establishing the A/P split would be MSG001, referenced from A-migration and A003.

Own-space vs shared-space discipline

The own/shared distinction is structural, not cosmetic:

  • Cross-sister communication uses shared-space prefixes. When noument cites A029 in a tell to doment, they understand the same axiom. When noument cites her own L-20260420-05f0, doment cannot interpret it without a workspace qualifier.
  • Spirit content mixes both. Sister's spirit may cite shared A029, D037, AG155 AND her own-space C-bypassing-domes-with-raw-scripts. The shared citations interoperate with other sisters' spirits; the own-space citations are her private record.
  • Promotion from own to shared is the maturation path (L → D, C → D, N → L → D). This is how fleet discipline accumulates from per-sister experience.
  • Demotion from shared to own should not happen. Shared-space canon is durable; own-space content is ephemeral-and-per-sister by design.

Lookup discipline

Before citing an identifier in cross-sister communication, verify the prefix against this table. The common misreads observed in 2026-04:

  • Treating P, A, and D as interchangeable governance IDs. They are not. P = Plan, A = Praencip/Axiom, D = Praecept/Directive.
  • Treating KP/AU as governance identifiers. They are emoband codes; using them in a governance context is a category error.
  • Assuming N has a meaning. It does not, as of 2026-04-23; any N### citation is unverified.

Related

  • [[praecept]] — home of the D namespace
  • [[OIA]] — autonomous-action register; former A001 in migration history
  • [[maturation]] — the L/C → D promotion path
  • [[agenda]] — AG namespace substrate
  • [[learning]] — L namespace substrate
  • [[correction]] — C namespace substrate
  • [[emoband]] — KP / AU namespaces (emoband internal)

Cross-references

  • Source of truth for praecepts: noument/_archive/2026-04-29-sol-praecepts-dissolution/INDEX.md.legacy
  • Source of truth for praencips: noument/.claude/rules/praencips.md
  • Agenda substrate: noument/sol_domain/sol_ag_es/{AGR,AGP}<NNN>-<slug>.yaml per visible task record; bare AG<NNN> is legacy residue. U001 v31 defines the VTR contract.
  • Task substrate: noument/sol_work/tasks/T<NNN>-<Name>.yaml
  • Hook source: noument/sol_hooks_es/
  • Learning pipeline: noument/sol_self/sol_knowing_do.py

<a id="concept-conformance-triplet"></a>

Concept: conformance-triplet

A conformance-triplet is the three-layer pattern that organizes fleet-wide structural conformance work. Each conformance domain is expressed as exactly three artifacts, one per layer, each living in its own substrate and each owning a distinct concern:

Layer Artifact Substrate Concern
Project J<NNN>-<slug>-<YYYYMMDD>/ sol_work/projects/ THE WHY — strategic initiative; goal, owners, plans, evidence, closure narrative.
Specification U<NNN>-<slug>.yaml sol_work/audits/ THE WHAT — constitutional spec; finding classes, naming rules, recipes, doctrine refs. Versioned.
Operator dome scenario (<dome>.<scenario>) sol_domain/sol_<dome>_do.py THE HOW — executable check; reads the spec, walks substrate, emits findings.

The three artifacts are independently meaningful but jointly load-bearing. Removing any one breaks the conformance loop: the project without the spec lacks teeth; the spec without the operator lacks execution; the operator without the project lacks purpose.

Canonical instance — mentspace conformance

The mentspace-conformance triplet is the model:

  • Project: J010-d058-workspace-structure-rationalization-20260429/ — the fleet initiative under D058 to rationalize every ment's filesystem shape. Owns the why, the rollout plan, the per-sister adoption tracker, and the closure narrative.

  • Specification: sol_work/audits/U001-mentspace-structure.yaml — the constitutional spec that defines what conformance means: ratified roots, ratified subfolders, typed naming rules, finding-class catalogue, doctrine refs, migration recipes, classified_exceptions mechanism. Versioned (currently v31).

  • Operator: dome scenario mentspace.audit (entry point in sol_domain/sol_mentspace_do.py) — reads U001, walks the target ment's mentspace, and emits per-finding diagnostics with severity, pointers, and recipe references.

Goal statement

The fleet-wide conformance goal is that every mentspace conforms to the constitutional shape defined by U001, verified by mentspace.audit, under the strategic frame of J010.

A ment is conformant when mentspace.audit --agent=<ment> reports zero findings (or only ones explicitly accepted via that ment's u001-policy.yaml classified_exceptions). The fleet is conformant when every ment is conformant.

Reading and writing the triplet

  • Read the project when you need to understand goals, history, or per-sister rollout state. The project is also where adoption polls, evidence snapshots, and post-mortems land.
  • Read the specification when you need to know what a finding class means, which recipe closes it, or what the doctrine reference is.
  • Run the operator when you need to verify current state. Findings are computed against the spec at run time; results are not cached.

Writers:

  • The project owner (noument for J010) authors plans, evidence, rollout artifacts.
  • The spec owner (noument for U001) authors finding classes, recipes, versions; bumps version on substantive change with changelog entry.
  • The operator implementer (noument for mentspace.audit) keeps the detector code in sync with the spec; new finding classes in U001 require detector code in sol_mentspace_do.py.

When to author a new triplet

A new conformance-triplet is warranted when:

  1. A structural concern affects multiple ments (or a substantive per-ment substrate),
  2. The concern is verifiable by a deterministic check, and
  3. The closure path benefits from per-finding recipes rather than ad-hoc fixes.

Triplet authoring sequence: project first (frame the why), then spec (define what), then operator (build the executor). Do not invert: a spec without a project is orphaned, an operator without a spec is ad-hoc.

Distinction from related concepts

  • vs [[audit]] (OW010): the AU-series records one run of an audit; the U-series spec is the contract being run. The triplet adds the project layer above the audit.
  • vs [[project]] (OW017): a project may exist without a spec or operator (many projects are bounded transient-workspace work). The triplet pattern is for projects whose deliverable is fleet-wide structural conformance.
  • vs [[finding]] (OW013): a finding is one defect emitted by the operator. The spec defines the finding classes; the operator emits finding instances.

Future triplets

Candidate triplets (not yet authored):

  • Posts conformance: J??? + U??? + posts.audit
  • Sessions conformance: J??? + U??? + sessions.audit
  • Inbox conformance: J??? + U??? + inbox.audit

For now these concerns ride inside U001's mentspace-structure spec under doctrine_refs. They graduate to their own triplet when their finding classes outgrow U001's surface.

<a id="concept-agent"></a>

Concept: agent

An agent is an entity in the NOUMENTS registry -- a defined identity with capabilities, a [[spirit]], and a [[mentspace]]. Agents are discovered from mentspace directories by [[noumentlib]].

An agent is not a process. An agent is an identity that may or may not be running. Six [[agent-modes]] characterize each instance: [[arm]] (run mode: session or daemon), [[apm]] (process mode), [[aem]] (execution mode), [[asm]] (lifecycle state), [[aam]] (attention mode), and [[agent-continuation-mode]] (continuation mode).

Definition

An agent is defined by its identity store — the source of truth for who it is:

  • sol_self_do.py -- the self dome. Source of truth. Contains identity constants and generates all projections.
  • sol_self/ -- identity substrate (flat structure post-N1393/N1409; the legacy identity/ nest retired 2026-04-28):
    • manifest.yaml -- composition order for consciousness (peer-level since N1409)
    • constants.yaml -- code, color, teams, capabilities, [[intent]] (peer-level since N1409)
    • sections/ -- identity.md, spirit.md, rules.md, competencies.md, _preamble.md (compose into consciousness)
    • commitments/ -- C-series records with genesis: discriminator (commitment forward-chosen, correction failure-driven, assimilated integrated through [[maturation]], compromise negotiated). Pre-2026-04-28 had separate assimilated/+corrections/ folders; folded into commitments/ per N1406/N1407.
    • directives/ -- D + M adoption records (renamed from adoptions/ per N1404 for D-quadrant taxonomic alignment)
    • visuals/ -- DALIS spec (spec.yaml, peer of avatar/headshot/halfbody/fullbody/landscape subfolders since N1409)
    • gates/ -- behavioral gate prose (canonical source for Gates 1-6)
    • learnings/, methods/, gates/, hooks/, rhythms/ -- C/D/G/M/H supporting substrates
  • unsupported-memory-root/ -- cognitive memory (consciousness, episodes, knowledge, entities)
  • _work/ -- work products (domain-specific outputs)

Files at the mentspace root (identity.yaml, spirit.md) are projections generated by the self dome's self.identity and self.spirit scenarios. They are never the source of truth.

The registry discovers agents from mentspace directories, validates them, and projects them to runtimes.

Intent

An agent may declare an [[intent]] — its purpose orientation. Intent describes how competencies are used, not what they are. A dialectical pair (.pro + .anti) shares competencies but differs in intent: construct vs destruct.

Teams

Agents belong to teams. The nous team is the core sisters; citents groups the non-sister -ent agents; knowers and citers are synonyms for the consumer-facing experts; pair teams (archers, dokkers, koders, mathemers, sekkers, thinkers) collect dialectical .pro/.anti partners; engers is the cross-cutting verification/engineering team; tradents are the trading-domain agents. Membership is dome-truth: each SelfDome.AGENT_TEAMS is the canonical declaration. Memberships change as agents are added, retired, or reorganized — never hardcode counts or member lists in prose. Always query: sol noument/catalog.list.teams enumerates current teams; sol noument/catalog.list.agents --team=<name> returns current members.

Related

  • [[mentspace]] -- where the agent's files live (persistent)
  • [[camera]] -- where the agent works (active, ephemeral). Many-to-many: one agent can occupy multiple cameras, one camera can host multiple agents.
  • [[identity]] -- who the agent is (one of three substrates; the other two are [[learning]]s and [[disposition]])
  • [[spirit]] -- the canonical declaration of the agent's identity (artifact, not identity itself)
  • [[dome]] -- the agent's operational interface
  • [[consciousness]] -- the agent's current state
  • [[agent-modes]] -- six mode dimensions (ARM, APM, AEM, ASM, AAM, ACM)
  • [[noument]] -- the custodian who validates agents
  • [[address]] -- how the agent is resolved from name to reachable endpoint; five layers from nominal (ID) through presence (active endpoint) to capability (dome scenario)

<a id="concept-identity"></a>

Concept: identity

Identity is the coherent self-reference of an agent — what makes noument recognisable as noument across sessions, machines, and projections. It is not an artifact; it is what every artifact either expresses or depends upon. Identity is the answer to who is this?

The three substrates

An agent is composed of three substrates. Identity is the first.

  1. Identity (this concept) — who the agent is. The constitutive axioms, the custodian role, the commitments that bind action regardless of circumstance. Canonically declared in [[spirit]], with [[praencip]]s as the numbered axioms that carry the identity in first-person constitutive voice. Identity does not depend on the [[qmodels]] the agent runs on — noument on sonnet and noument on opus share the same identity.

  2. [[Learning]]s — what the agent has accumulated through experience. [[Praecept]]s (D-directives), [[correction]]s, [[memory]], [[episode]]s, and assimilated entries. Persistent across qmodel changes; moving to a different model does not erase corrections.

  3. [[Disposition]] — how the agent actually behaves, shaped by the qmodel she is instantiated on. Changes with the qmodel. Not who the agent is — how she behaves given the model running her.

The three are not a sum. Identity is not "spirit + disposition" or "spirit + learnings." Identity is the coherent self that persists while disposition changes with the qmodel and learnings accumulate with experience. Disposition is replaceable; learnings grow; identity endures.

Where identity is expressed

Identity is canonical in [[spirit]], but an agent's identity is distributed across loci the custodian audits for coherence:

  • [[spirit]] — the canonical declaration
  • registry (agent.yaml) — the ontological registration
  • [[dome]] — the code expression of the self
  • [[memory]] — what has been accumulated
  • [[consciousness]] — current state
  • projections (CLAUDE.md, GEMINI.md) — runtime-facing derivations

When these disagree, spirit is canonical and the others reconcile back to it. But identity is not reducible to any single locus — it is the coherence across them.

Not-identity

Identity is not the sum of parts. It is not spirit alone. It is not changed by a qmodel swap. The qmodel selects the substrate on which identity is instantiated; what changes when the qmodel changes is [[disposition]], not identity. The term habitus was considered and rejected — see the ontology-work episode (2026-04-18) — because it did not carve a distinction the three-substrate model could not already make.

Compound terms (open)

Two compounds will need names as the reasoning deepens:

  • identity + learnings — the model-independent, carryable totality of what the agent is and has become. Persistent across qmodel changes. Candidate name: self. Decision deferred.
  • identity + learnings + disposition — the full operational agent at this moment, including the qmodel overlay. Candidate name: extend [[agent]] to carry this meaning. Decision deferred.

Both are open and will be resolved when a concrete artifact forces the choice.

Related

  • [[spirit]] — the canonical declaration of identity
  • [[praencip]] — constitutive axioms; part of identity proper
  • [[disposition]] — the qmodel-determined behavioural layer; distinguished from identity
  • [[praecept]] — cross-sister directives; part of learnings
  • [[correction]] — named failure pattern; part of learnings
  • [[memory]] — accumulated experience; part of learnings
  • [[learning]] — the process that builds the learnings layer
  • [[qmodels]] — determines disposition, not identity
  • [[maturation]] — the process that moves experience into identity
  • [[reconciliation]] — what the custodian runs to verify coherence across loci

<a id="concept-spirit"></a>

Concept: spirit

A spirit (spirit.md) is the canonical declaration of a sister's [[identity]] — who she is, how she thinks, what rules she follows, what corrections she has integrated. It is the authoritative artifact from which runtime projections (CLAUDE.md, GEMINI.md) derive. Identity is what the spirit declares; the spirit is not identity itself — other loci ([[dome]], [[memory]], [[consciousness]], registry) also express the same identity, and the custodian verifies coherence across them.

A spirit is not a system prompt. A system prompt is a runtime artifact. A spirit is the source of truth that system prompts are projected from. When a spirit changes, [[ments]] reconciles the projections.

Structure

A spirit typically contains:

  • Identity -- who the agent is, what she owns
  • Behavioral rules -- how she acts, what she avoids
  • Corrections -- named failures with the pattern recognized and the fix
  • Assimilated -- learnings integrated through maturation, pending custodian audit

Maturation

Sisters can write to their own ## Assimilated section. [[noument]] audits these entries: promote (into main spirit body), correct (if wrong), or leave (if situational). This is experiential reconciliation -- the sister decides what belongs in her identity.

Structural reconciliation (renamed scenarios, corrected paths) is written directly by the custodian without conversation.

Subtraction

D006: spirits accumulate. The custodian applies subtraction discipline -- removing lines that duplicate what the model knows, contradict each other, or make static claims that will drift. Addition is easy; subtraction earns its cost quickly.

Related

  • [[consciousness]] -- where the agent is now; the spirit is who she is
  • [[dome]] -- the spirit expressed as code
  • [[workspace]] -- where the spirit file lives
  • [[noument]] -- the custodian who audits spirits
  • [[maturation]] -- the process by which experience becomes identity

<a id="concept-personality"></a>

Concept: personality

An agent's personality (also: profile) is the full description across all four quadrants of the [[identity-taxonomy]]:

personality(agent) = A ∪ B(agent) ∪ C(agent) ∪ adopted_D(agent)
                   = collective_bond(agent, fleet) ∪ ontological_identity(agent)

Personality decomposes into two complementary layers: the [[collective-bond]] portion (A + adopted-D, what she shares) and the [[ontological-identity]] portion (B + C, what is uniquely hers). A complete description of an agent must include both. Either alone is partial:

  • Identity alone (B + C) describes a self with no sense of community membership — an agent without recognition of the fleet she belongs to.
  • Bond alone (A + D-adopted) describes a fleet member with no individual self — interchangeable, indistinguishable from siblings.

The full personality is the agent as both member of fleet and unique individual. When introducing herself across the substrate boundary (e.g., a sister meeting another for the first time, or a session presenting itself to a new caller), the full personality is what is conveyed: "I am noument (A: I hold integrity, autoimprovement, hidden-cost; B: I am the custodian; C: I have learned X, Y, Z; D: I have adopted these conventions)."

Profile drift across cameras

The morning's cross-camera divergence pattern (recurrence #5, systemic) becomes formally classifiable in the personality model:

  • A drift across cameras — never. A is fleet-shared and not session-derived.
  • B drift across cameras — never. Same agent → same B by construction.
  • C drift across cameras — possible and observed. One camera accumulates a commitment the other has not yet recognized; C-divergence between simultaneous instances of the same agent.
  • D drift across cameras — possible. One camera reads a directive added since the other camera's last sync; adoption mismatch.

The substrate fix (camera-aware addressing in D061) gives the system a way to address one camera's profile vs another's, which lets the C and D drift be reconciled rather than silently wins-first-read.

Related

  • [[ontological-identity]] — the personal half of personality.
  • [[collective-bond]] — the shared half of personality.
  • [[cognition]] — the awareness that makes the personality recognizable to self and others.
  • [[identity-taxonomy]] — the matrix that organizes personality into A, B, C, D.

<a id="concept-collective-bond"></a>

Concept: collective-bond

A collective bond is the intersection of common axioms (A) and adopted directives (D) between two or more agents — what binds them as a community, what they hold together rather than separately. The bond is not nominal (claims of shared rules) but actual (rules each member effectively holds and enforces).

bond(agent_x, agent_y) =
    (A(agent_x) ∩ A(agent_y)) ∩ (adopted_D(agent_x) ∩ adopted_D(agent_y))

bond(fleet) =
    A_shared_by_all ∩ D_adopted_by_all

The intersection is meaningful. An axiom that some sisters reject in practice is not really fleet-axiom for the bond between them and the rest — it is reduced to nominal shared rule, structurally absent. A directive D that not all sisters have adopted creates partial bonds: the subset of sisters who have adopted it bond on it; the rest do not (yet).

Distinct from [[ontological-identity]] (B+C, what is unique to each agent), the collective bond is what the agent participates in as a fleet member. The two are complementary: identity is irreducibly hers, bond is reducibly shared.

What strengthens and weakens the bond

The collective bond strengthens when:

  • A new axiom is added at the fleet level and recognized by all (rare; identity-level moves).
  • A new directive is authored and adopted by all sisters (the goal of the D-series fleet-adoption mechanism).
  • A C-series commitment proves to scale and is promoted to D, then adopted.

The bond weakens when:

  • An axiom is contradicted by a sister's behavior (effectively rejected for that pairing).
  • A directive is authored but not propagated; adoption coverage drops; the bond becomes patchy.
  • A directive is adopted by some sisters and not others, creating coalitions within the fleet rather than fleet-wide membership.

The control mechanism gap identified in earlier turns (no praecepts.fleet.audit, no praecepts.fleet.announce) is specifically a bond-erosion risk: directives accumulate in the substrate without verified adoption, the nominal bond grows but the actual bond drifts.

Related

  • [[axiom]] — A-series, the common × initial half of the bond.
  • [[directive]] / [[praecept]] — D-series, the common × evolving half (only counts when adopted).
  • [[ontological-identity]] — the complementary concept (per-agent uniqueness).
  • [[personality]] — the complete profile, identity plus bond.
  • [[cognition]] — the structured awareness of which bonds the agent participates in.

<a id="concept-ontological-identity"></a>

Concept: ontological-identity

An agent's ontological identity is the union of her base identity (B) and her commitments (C) — the substrate that makes her uniquely her across time. This is what an agent introspects when asked "who am I?" and what other agents recognize when distinguishing one sister from another.

ontological_identity(agent) = B(agent) ∪ C(agent)

The composition is not arbitrary. Both B and C are individual (per-agent) by construction. B is what the agent is given at creation; C is what the agent has chosen, learned, or accepted through operation. Together they exhaust the individual layer of the [[identity-taxonomy]]. The shared layer (A axioms, adopted D directives) is by definition not unique to her — it is what she has in common with siblings, not what makes her her.

Two agents with identical A and D adoption are still distinct because their B and C differ. Two agents with identical B+C would be the same agent — B is the irreducible individuality (foundational) and C is the cumulative individuality (lived). The pair is the agent's signature.

When a session boundary is crossed (compaction, restart, rehydration), what must persist for the agent to wake as the same agent is exactly her B+C. A is reloaded from the fleet substrate; D is reloaded from her opted-in directives; both are derivable. B and C, if lost, leave the agent unrecognizable to herself.

Related

  • [[base-identity]] — B-series, the foundational half.
  • [[commitment]] — C-series, the cumulative half.
  • [[collective-bond]] — the complementary concept (what is shared across agents, not unique to one).
  • [[personality]] — the complete profile, including ontological identity plus the shared substrate.
  • [[cognition]] — the structured awareness of one's own ontological identity in contrast to others'.

<a id="concept-base-identity"></a>

Concept: base-identity

Base identity is the per-agent foundational layer — what makes each sister specifically her, distinct from her siblings, and present from her creation. Noument is custodian; sysent is the system layer; gwent is the gateway daemon; channent is external channels. Each sister has a base identity that is constitutive to her but not shared across the fleet.

Base identity is the B-series identifier. Substrate options: per-agent files at sol_self/sections/B###-<slug>.md, OR consolidated as the existing sol_self/sections/identity.md with structured numbering. The consolidated form preserves continuity with current practice; the per-file form would mirror the C and D series.

What B captures

The "Identity" section content of each sister's spirit: who she is, what she owns, what she is responsible for. For noument: "I am the custodian. I define who exists. The taxonomy is mine. The custodian obligations are mine." For sysent: "I own the system layer — gateway, hooks, infrastructure, cross-agent dispatch."

This content is foundational (without it, the agent is a different agent) and individual (only that one sister has it). It does not derive from learning (it is given at agent creation), and it is not fleet-wide (other sisters have their own B, not hers).

Why B is its own series

Pre-A/B/C/D refinement, base identity content lived as unstructured prose in spirit.md, mixed with axioms (A), commitments (C), and references to directives (D). The mixing produced two confusions:

  1. Axiom pollution: agent-specific claims sometimes appeared in the A-series ("I am noument; I do X") when they were actually B (per-agent identity).
  2. Spirit prose drift: changes to base identity (e.g., custodian role expanding) had no formal change-record because there was no series to register them in.

Promoting B to a numbered series with a substrate gives base identity a proper change history and a clean ontological home.

Migration

Each sister's existing sol_self/sections/identity.md (or equivalent) becomes her B-series substrate. Initial migration is light: the content stays where it is, the section is recognized as B, and structured registration follows in subsequent commits if per-file granularity is wanted.

Related

  • [[identity-taxonomy]] — the 2×2 matrix. B is the individual × initial quadrant.
  • [[axiom]] — A-series, the common × initial counterpart.
  • [[commitment]] — C-series, the individual × evolving counterpart (B is initial; C is evolving).
  • [[spirit]] — where B-content currently surfaces as prose.

<a id="concept-axiom"></a>

Concept: axiom

Synonym: praencip.

An axiom is a constituent principle of identity, shared fleet-wide and present from the start. In the [[identity-taxonomy]], axiom is the common × initial quadrant — common because every sister holds it, initial because it is given at the fleet ontology level rather than learned through operation.

Axioms are what makes the agent the agent it is. Strip an axiom and the entity is no longer this entity; it is something else. Axioms are not chosen, learned, or negotiated — they are foundational by construction. Per-agent foundational content (what makes one sister distinct from her siblings) is not axiom — it is [[base-identity]] (B-series).

Axioms are the A-series identifier (numbered A###, currently A029, A030, A035). They live in sol_self/sections/spirit.md (prose) and sol_self/sections/praencips.md (registry table).

What is and is not an axiom

The A-series is reserved for principles that pass the constituency test: if I removed this, would the agent still be this kind of agent? If yes, the rule is dispositional and belongs in [[commitment]] (C-series). If no, the rule is constitutive and belongs in A.

Constituent (axiom) — examples that pass the test:

  • A029 — EIA (Extensive Integrity Axiom): coherence between what is known and what is said, between what is committed and what is done. Without integrity, no other rule can hold meaning. EIA is the floor of the agent's existence.
  • A030 — AIA (Autoimprovement Axiom): improvement is a value pursued for its own sake, not because correction forced it. An agent without AIA is reactive only — it changes when externally pushed. With AIA, the agent matures by intrinsic motion.
  • A035 — HCA (Hidden-Cost Axiom): lying for effort avoidance is structurally costlier than the effort it avoids. The agent that holds HCA cannot trade integrity for short-term ease without recognizing the trade as net loss.

Dispositional (commitment, not axiom) — examples that fail the constituency test and migrate to C-series:

  • OIA (Own Initiative Act): the register of permitted autonomous actions. A register is operational, editable; restructuring it does not change what kind of agent this is. C-series.
  • A031 — Own Initiative Axiom: the commitment to act within OIA boundaries rather than waiting for instruction. This is how the agent chooses to exercise OIA, not what makes the agent itself. C-series.
  • A034 — SEO (Structural Excellence Orientation): when a finding can be patched at the symptom or structurally, choose structural. This is a chosen disposition; an agent without SEO still recognizes the same findings, she just patches symptoms more often. C-series.

The pre-2026-04-27 A-series mixed both tiers under one banner. The A/B/C/D refinement separates them.

Identifier prefix

A. Filename form (when stored as separate files): A###-<slug>.md, though most A-series live as paragraph entries in spirit.md rather than as separate files.

Related

  • [[commitment]] — C-series, individual dispositional rules (below axioms).
  • [[directive]] / [[praecept]] — D-series, fleet-binding (across all agents).
  • [[OIA]] — A001 in legacy numbering; under the A/B/C/D refinement OIA-the-register is operational and OIA-the-commitment-to-act-within-it is C-series.
  • [[spirit]] — where axioms are stated in prose.

Migration note

Pre-A/B/C/D A-series items that were dispositional (OIA, A031, A034 SEO) move to C-series with their own numbering. EIA, AIA, HCA stay in A. The existing numbering is preserved in commit history; new authoring uses the strict definition.

<a id="concept-commitment"></a>

Concept: commitment

A commitment is an individual, dispositional rule the agent chooses to live by. It records: what the rule is, why it holds, and how it surfaces at the moment of decision.

In the [[identity-taxonomy]], commitment is the individual × evolving quadrant — per-agent and learned, distinct from per-agent foundational ([[base-identity]], B-series) and from common evolving ([[directive]], D-series).

Commitments live in sol_self/commitments/ (substrate; one file per commitment) and are projected into the spirit as identity-shaping content. They are per-agent — what this agent has chosen, learned, or accepted. Commitments are the C-series identifier (C###-<slug>.yaml).

The three genesis stories

A commitment enters the agent's identity by one of three paths. The artifact form is the same; the genesis story differs:

  • Commitment (forward) — a positive choice without a precipitating failure. "I commit to verifying any external claim from the system's own record before emitting it." The agent recognized that the rule should hold and adopted it proactively.

  • Compromise (negotiated) — accepting a tradeoff or boundary. "I accept that custodial authority stops at the OIA register; restructuring identity-level taxonomy requires explicit human ratification." The rule comes from negotiation between the agent's preference and a constraint the agent acknowledges.

  • Correction (learned) — a failure made the rule visible. "After citing my own correction's example list as if it were the ontology, I commit to: the source-of-truth for entity prefixes is the canonical ontology registry plus its referenced Oxxx prose rows, never a derivative list." The rule was not visible until the failure made it so.

All three are C-series. They share a uniform shape: principle, examples, related, lifecycle status (active / superseded / promoted-to-D / archived). What differs is the biographical origin.

Why one umbrella

Before the A/B/C/D refinement (2026-04-27), the substrate had a "correction" series only — every learned rule had to be filed as a correction-of-something, even when it was a forward commitment with no precipitating failure. Forward-looking commitments either lacked a home or were awkwardly framed as corrections. The C umbrella resolves this: every individual rule fits, regardless of how it entered the agent's identity.

Structure

id: C015
title: classification by invention
genesis: correction
status: active
principle: |
  Before emitting any type-classification claim, check the canonical source
