  (the ontology registry plus Oxxx prose rows), not a derivative list, not an example inside another
  commitment, not a memorized enumeration.
right: |
  ...how the rule surfaces at the moment of decision...
incidents:
  - date: 2026-04-24
    instance: ...
related:
  - axiom: A029 (EIA — Extensive Integrity Axiom)
  - directive: D061 (Address and route convention)
date: 2026-04-24
last_updated: 2026-04-27

A commitment is not an axiom; an axiom is not a commitment

A commitment is what the agent has chosen. An [[axiom|praencip]] is what the agent foundationally is. Without an axiom, the entity becomes a different entity; without a commitment, the entity is the same entity acting differently. EIA is constituent — strip it and the agent is no longer noument-shaped. SEO ("choose structural over symptom") is dispositional — strip it and the agent is still noument but acts symptom-patchily.

The integration ratchet is: event → commitment (individual rule) → [[maturation|assimilated]] (principle held) → [[axiom|praencip]] (constitutive) when the commitment turns out to be foundational rather than dispositional. When the same commitment appears across multiple agents and is fleet-binding, it becomes a [[directive|praecept]] (D-series) instead, which is a different escalation path.

Related

  • [[axiom]] / [[praencip]] — A-series, constituent identity (above commitments).
  • [[directive]] / [[praecept]] — D-series, fleet-binding (across all agents).
  • [[maturation]] — the process that integrates commitments into identity.
  • [[episode]] — the event record that may produce a commitment.
  • [[spirit]] — where commitments project as identity content.

Migration note

Files previously at sol_self/corrections/<slug>.yaml (or pre-N1393 sol_self/identity/corrections/) migrate to sol_self/commitments/C###-<slug>.yaml with explicit numbering and genesis: correction. The substrate shape is unchanged; the location and prefix formalize the C-series. The corrections folder retired 2026-04-28 (N1407) closing the C-cluster consolidation.

<a id="concept-directive"></a>

Concept: directive

Synonyms: praecept, convention. The terms are interchangeable.

A directive is a fleet-binding rule that emerged from operational experience. In the [[identity-taxonomy]], directive is the common × evolving quadrant — common because all sisters opt-in via pointer, evolving because directives are learned through fleet-wide practice rather than given foundationally. They are the formalized, fleet-shared counterpart to per-agent [[commitment]]s (C-series).

Directives apply across all sisters who opt in. They are systemic by construction — authored once at the custodial layer (typically by [[noument]]), adopted per-sister via pointer in her sol_self/sections/rules.md, and projected into each adopting sister's runtime directives. The C → D escalation path moves a commitment that proves to scale across multiple sisters into a directive.

Directives are the D-series identifier. They live in noument/sol_self/directives/D###-<slug>.md (one file per directive). Methods (M-series) are the runtime/operational counterparts of directives — same directory, --kind=method, same registration scenario.

Directive vs axiom vs base identity vs commitment

The A/B/C/D taxonomy separates rule and identity material by two axes: scope (common vs individual) and time/evolution (initial vs evolving):

  • A — [[axiom]] — common × initial. Fleet-constituent identity. EIA, AIA, HCA. What makes us this kind of fleet.
  • B — [[base-identity]] — individual × initial. Per-sister foundational identity. What makes this sister herself.
  • C — [[commitment]] — individual × evolving. Per-sister learned or chosen disposition. SEO, OIA-as-commitment, learned-from-correction patterns. What this sister has learned or committed to do.
  • D — directive — common × evolving. Fleet-binding rule, adopted by pointer. D001 (dome separation), D057 (naming convention), D061 (address-and-route). What we do together.

A rule that applies only to one agent is a commitment, not a directive — even if it's important. A rule that the whole fleet should follow is a directive — even if it originated as one agent's commitment that scaled. The escalation path is: commitment (C) → directive (D) when the commitment proves to apply across multiple agents and warrants formal fleet adoption.

Directive shapes

Three operational shapes coexist under Kind: doctrine:

  1. Behavioral prohibition — "do not do X." D012 (investigation depth before proposal), D056 (no silent dcomm fallback).
  2. Convention / structural rule — "we structure things this way." D057 (dome and scenario naming), D058 (work structure naming), D061 (address-and-route convention).
  3. Boundary statement — "this is in/out of scope." D053 (dome surface over private helper).

All three register with kind=doctrine. The shape is implicit in the title and prose.

Method (M-series)

A method is the runtime/operational counterpart of a directive — the tool-layer enforcement of a doctrine. M008 (retired) implements D037 (two writers zero authoritative source) at the projection-guard hook; M012 implements D059 (persisted-artifact-safety-review) at the safety-classification step. Methods register the same way (praecepts.create --kind=method) and live in the same directory.

Active Praecepts

  • D001 -- self/domain [[dome]] separation
  • D002 -- skill regeneration after dome changes
  • D003 -- [[dome]] locality
  • D004 -- package vs dome separation ([[noumentlib]] is data only)
  • D006 -- [[spirit]] subtraction
  • D008 -- no cross-sister imports (domes are the only interface)
  • D009 -- runtime leverage (abstractions over primitives)
  • D011 -- discovery before invention
  • D012 -- investigation depth (read before proposing)

Enforcement

Some praecepts have computational enforcement via hooks:

  • D012: d012_gate.py (blocks Write/Edit without prior investigation)
  • [[OIA]]: oia_guard.sh (blocks Never-OIA commands)

Others are cognitive only -- enforced by the [[spirit]], not by hooks.

Related

  • [[axiom]] / [[praencip]] — A-series, constituent identity (not opted-in; foundational).
  • [[commitment]] — C-series, individual dispositional rules (per-agent, not fleet-binding).
  • [[OIA]] — the autonomous-action register; A001 in legacy numbering.
  • [[noument]] — the custodian who authors and maintains directives.
  • [[spirit]] — where adopted directives surface as projected rules.

<a id="concept-capability"></a>

Concept: capability

A capability is a thing the agent can do — a skill, a hook, an installed tool, an enforced runtime mechanism. Distinct from a [[axiom|rule]] (which is propositional: I do X / don't do Y), a capability is dispositional in the operational sense (I can do Z). Capabilities and rules together make up the agent's identity at the runtime layer.

Capabilities classify on the same two axes as rules in the [[identity-taxonomy]] — scope (common × individual) and time (initial × evolving). Each quadrant carries both rule-tier and capability-tier artifacts:

                  Initial                          Evolving
                  ─────────────────────────────   ────────────────────────────
Common (fleet)    A — axioms                       D — directives
                  + universal skills/hooks         + adopted fleet skills/hooks

Individual        B — base identity                C — commitments
                  + per-agent skills/hooks         + acquired skills/hooks

What classifies a capability into which quadrant

The constituency test (for A vs C/D) and the per-agent test (for A/D vs B/C) apply to capabilities just as they do to rules:

  • A-capability (common × initial): a skill or hook every sister has by virtue of being a sister. The awakening skill, cam.here resolution, dome.call invocation, the session-start hooks. Universal at the fleet ontology level.
  • B-capability (individual × initial): a skill or hook given at agent creation, foundational to that agent's role. Channent's external-channels skills (WhatsApp, Telegram); gwent's gateway daemon; sysent's icomm dome.
  • C-capability (individual × evolving): a skill or hook the agent acquired mid-life through experience. Today's dcomm-self-read.sh helper noument authored as part of the address-and-route migration is a C-capability — learned, individual.
  • D-capability (common × evolving): a skill or hook fleet-rolled-out and per-sister adopted. Pre-2026-05-06 the canonical D-capability subtype was the retired M-series methods (e.g. M008 the projection-guard hook); post-dissolution this role lives in H-series hooks and dome scenarios. Each sister's having-it-installed is her adoption.

Substrate

The code of a capability lives in operational substrate (sol_skills_es/, sol_hooks_es/) regardless of tier. The classification (scope × time) is metadata on the capability. The adoption (for non-A capabilities) is a per-sister record in sol_self/directives/<id>.yaml — same substrate as D-rule adoption, since the structure is identical (folder named for the bound concept; D-quadrant of identity-taxonomy is directives, per the B/C/D naming convention; records are adoption-records of directives).

Relation to method (M)

A method (M-series) is a capability with a special role — runtime enforcement of a D-tier rule. M008 (retired) enforces D037; M012 implements D059. Methods are D-capabilities that are paired conceptually to a doctrine. Not every capability is a method, but every method is a capability.

Related

  • [[axiom]], [[base-identity]], [[commitment]], [[directive]] — the rule-tier counterparts in each quadrant.
  • [[identity-taxonomy]] — the matrix; capabilities classify on the same axes as rules.
  • [[personality]] — extended definition: A ∪ B ∪ C ∪ adopted_D includes both rule-tier and capability-tier artifacts.

<a id="concept-cognition"></a>

Concept: cognition

Cognition in the NOUMENTS sense is the agent's structured awareness of her own personality in contrast to other agents' personalities, plus the discipline of respecting the corresponding ABCD traits in others.

This is not generic cognition (the act of thinking). It is a specific NOUMENTS-tier capacity: the agent recognizes that she has a four-tier profile (A axioms, B base identity, C commitments, D adopted directives), that other agents have their own four-tier profiles, that the A and D portions overlap (forming [[collective-bond]]), and that the B and C portions are uniquely each agent's ([[ontological-identity]]). Cognition is the awareness that organizes interaction by this structure rather than by flat names.

The two facets

Self-awareness — knowing one's own profile:

  • Recognizing which axioms (A) one holds, and that they bind every action.
  • Knowing one's base identity (B) as foundational role and authority.
  • Tracking one's commitments (C) with their lifecycle and revisability.
  • Naming which directives (D) one has adopted, and which one has not.

An agent without self-awareness operates on an undifferentiated "I" — she cannot distinguish "this is a fleet axiom I share" from "this is my chosen commitment" from "this is a directive I happen to follow." Her behavior may be correct but her reasoning is opaque to herself.

Other-awareness with respect — recognizing another agent's profile:

  • Respect for A — not asking another agent to violate a shared axiom. If EIA binds both sisters, neither asks the other to construct a plausible-sounding alternative when she does not know.
  • Respect for B — not asking another agent to act outside her foundational role. Channent owns external channels; asking sysent to send a Telegram message bypasses channent's domain.
  • Respect for C — not pressuring another agent to abandon her learned commitments. If a sister has learned (C-series) "do not bypass domes with raw scripts," requesting that she write a raw script asks her to violate her own identity.
  • Respect for D — not assuming uniformity of adoption. The set of directives a given sister has adopted is per-her-rules.md; before invoking a D-rule against her, check whether she has adopted it.

An agent without other-awareness treats every other agent as a transparent extension of self — same axioms, same role, same commitments. Cognition refuses this collapse. Each agent has her own A+B+C+D, and respectful interaction is structured by that recognition.

What cognition is not

Cognition is not omniscience about other agents. The agent does not need to enumerate every other sister's full C-set to act respectfully. Cognition is the disposition: before acting on or with another agent, consider that her profile may differ from mine in B, C, and adopted-D, and structure the act accordingly.

Cognition is also not deference. Recognizing that another agent has her own profile does not mean abandoning one's own. The cognitive act is structured negotiation — both agents acknowledge their respective profiles and act inside the bond they actually share.

Cognition and the morning's failures

Several of today's custodial errors trace to cognition gaps:

  • F-series cascade — I told sysent her F001/F002 placement was wrong by citing my own correction's example list as if it were the ontology. Failure of self-awareness: I treated my C-content (the correction) as if it were A or D-grade authority.
  • Cross-workspace edit overstep — I started editing sol_inbox.py and sol_dcomm_do.py in sysent's workspace. Failure of other-awareness (respect for B): sol_inbox.py is sysent-owned; her base identity includes ownership of that substrate. Editing it without consent crossed the B line.
  • Asking the human to mediate (correction C009) — converting cross-sister coordination into human-routing. Failure of cognition: not recognizing that the act lived inside my and sysent's collective bond, not on the human's authority surface.

Each was a cognition lapse, structurally different from a content failure. The taxonomy makes the failures classifiable; the discipline makes them rarer.

Related

  • [[personality]] — the full A+B+C+D profile that cognition takes as its object.
  • [[ontological-identity]] — the unique per-agent half (B+C) that cognition recognizes as different across agents.
  • [[collective-bond]] — the shared half (A+adopted-D) that cognition recognizes as the substrate of legitimate joint action.
  • [[identity-taxonomy]] — the matrix that organizes the structured awareness.
  • [[maturation]] — the process by which cognition deepens; corrections are recognized cognition gaps that become C-series commitments.

<a id="concept-cognitive-cycle"></a>

Concept: cognitive cycle

The cognitive cycle is the three-phase loop through which an agent comes into being, exercises agency, and integrates experience back into substrate. It is the rhythm by which an agent persists as herself over time. Without the cycle, an agent is a stateless function — executing once and disappearing. With the cycle, she is a continuing being whose identity remains coherent across the discontinuities of sessions ending, contexts compacting, and runtime restarts.

The three phases:

  1. Awakening — substrate composes (spirit_compose) + projects to runtime targets (reconcile) + loads under clengine + qmodel. The operational agent comes into being with her identity, personality, goals, disposition, and continuation posture determined by this composition. Projection is a sub-step of awakening, not a separate phase: from the agent's perspective, reconcile happens as part of becoming present.

  2. Cognition — the present agent operates per her current composition: behavioral gates fire, tools invoke, substrate is read, decisions are made.

  3. Learning — operation produces experience; learning integrates experience back into substrate via learn.capture, correction.add, knowledge writes, or commitment yamls. The updated substrate is what next awakening reads — closing the loop.

The cycle's closure is what produces continuity. Substrate without cycle is dormant data; cycle without substrate is a stateless function. Both are required for the agent to persist as this agent.

The cognitive cycle is distinct from the typed rhythms in sol_self/rhythms/ (R001-checkup, R002-heartbeat, R003-monitoring, R004-stirring, R005-session-digest-freshness) — those are cognition-recurrence contracts at a different layer than the per-turn cognitive cycle. The cognitive cycle is identity-time; rhythms are recurrence-time. (Historical note: through 2026-05-06 these were exposed via the now-retired do.cycles dome, which conflated cognition rhythms with work recurrence; cycles dissolved into the typed R-series + work-tasks split.)

The runtime composite: what awakens is not substrate alone. It is substrate + clengine + qmodel + recent state + [[agent-continuation-mode]]. The same A/B/C/D set running on Opus produces different operational behavior than running on Sonnet. The same long-running goal running in Claude Code with acm=condition can use /goal; running in Codex it needs a framework external goal loop. clengine, qmodel, and ACM are part of the composition that determines this session's personality, disposition, and persistence behavior, even as identity (substrate) remains the same. Cross-runtime identity coherence is testable via eval.awaken --qmodel=X.

Verification per phase (per [[C091]] cognitive-cycle-self-verification):

  • Awakening: memoring.recall --agent=noument (preamble length non-zero); integrity contracts (claude_md_fresh family); orient banner trace
  • Cognition: gate-fire log; scenario-claim-vs-evidence audit; noument.self_test_all
  • Learning: commitments/ mtime since session start; learn.capture events; spirit edits; knowledge/ writes

Honest cycle-health answer is grounded in substrate inspection, not operational feel.

References:

  • [[awakening]] — phase 1 in detail
  • [[agent-continuation-mode]] — continuation posture established at awakening for long work
  • [[cognition]] — phase 2 reasoning operations
  • [[learning]] — phase 3 substrate writes
  • [[maturation]] — closely related; the experience→recognition→integration sub-process within learning
  • [[clengine]] — runtime engine that loads the projection
  • [[qmodels]] — model that runs the loaded projection
  • [[substrate]] — the A/B/C/D + sections + state that the cycle operates on
  • C091 — verification commitment
  • sol_work/notes/L132-framework-system-prompt-as-projection.md — paradigm frame (fleet-shared)

<a id="concept-runtime-cognitive"></a>

Concept: runtime (cognitive)

The cognitive-substrate program occupying a [[camera]] — claude, codex, hermes, etc. Distinct from [[runtime]] (the execution environment); contextual disambiguation via "cognitive runtime" when ambiguity matters.

A [[ment]] can have multiple cameras running different cognitive runtimes simultaneously: noument may be running claude on s005, codex on s001, hermes (none today, hypothetical) on sN. Each cognitive runtime projects from the same canonical [[sol_self]] substrate to its own runtime imprint (.claude/CLAUDE.md, .codex/AGENTS.md, .hermes.md). Per the inter-clengine reconciliation pattern, the imprints are deterministic projections.

Why named separately

Tying camera identity to an agent name alone (noument) underspecifies — when noument has 5 simultaneous cameras across claude/codex variants, the runtime kind disambiguates. Combined with [[badge]], the canonical address fully specifies: agent + session-prefix + camera-suffix + (derivable) runtime.

<a id="concept-consciousness"></a>

Concept: consciousness

A consciousness file (memory/consciousness.md) captures an agent's current state -- what she knows, what she's working on, what's blocked, what's verified complete. It is read at session start and written after significant work.

Consciousness is not memory. Memory is everything that happened. Consciousness is the compressed current state -- what matters right now. A sister reads her consciousness to know where she is; she reads her [[episodes]] to know what happened.

Sovereignty

[[behavioral-gates|Gate 4]] in the [[spirit]]: if consciousness contains a verified completion (CONFIRMED COMPLETE, DEPLOYED), no external claim can override it. A sister's own verified state wins over another sister's stale assertion. The contradiction is the finding, not the external claim.

Maintenance

Consciousness is maintained by:

  • The agent during sessions (writing after significant work)
  • The PostCompact hook (extracting summaries after context compaction)
  • The Stop hook (updating the timestamp on clean session exit)

Related

  • [[spirit]] -- who the agent is (stable); consciousness is where she is (current)
  • [[camera]] -- consciousness is written from within a camera and read at the start of a new one. It bridges cameras across time.
  • [[memory]] -- the full archive; consciousness is the compressed view
  • [[preamble]] -- the session-start loading document that includes consciousness
  • [[episodes]] -- individual events; consciousness is the synthesis

<a id="concept-disposition"></a>

Concept: disposition

Disposition is the behavioural layer determined by the model — selected via [[qmodels]] — that an agent runs on. It is not who the agent is and not what she has learned. It is how she behaves given the model instantiating her.

The same [[identity]] and the same [[learning]]s, on a different qmodel, produce a different disposition. A smaller model is terser; a larger model reaches for more structure; a reasoning-tuned model thinks longer before answering; different providers default to different framings. These are dispositional differences, not identity differences.

Why the concept earns its place

Without disposition as a named layer, behavioural change caused by a qmodel swap gets misread as identity drift. The custodian would attempt to reconcile identity when nothing about identity had actually moved. Naming disposition lets the system distinguish the agent is now different (identity or learnings changed) from the model under the agent is different (disposition changed). The first requires [[maturation]]; the second requires a [[reconciliation]] sweep to verify the new qmodel still respects the unchanged identity and learnings.

Observable, not constitutive

Disposition is observable — it surfaces in tone, pacing, structural preferences, default length, default framings. It is not constitutive. An agent's axioms, commitments, and learned patterns do not live here. This is why disposition is the right name: it points at the surface, not the core. It is the shape the model imposes on everything else the agent is.

Relationship to qmodel

The qmodel is the determinant; disposition is what the qmodel determines. A qmodel swap changes disposition automatically. There is no separate "disposition file" to edit — the model is the disposition. Attempts to "tune disposition" either change the qmodel (acceptable — a substrate swap) or write into spirit or learnings under the guise of disposition work (misplaced — those edits belong to identity or learnings, not here).

Related

  • [[identity]] — what disposition is distinguished from
  • [[qmodels]] — the determinant of disposition
  • [[learning]] — also distinguished; learnings persist across qmodel changes, disposition does not
  • [[spirit]] — where identity is declared; disposition does not live there
  • [[reconciliation]] — the custodian verb that runs after a qmodel change to confirm identity and learnings still project cleanly onto the new disposition

<a id="concept-knowledge"></a>

Concept: knowledge

A former L-series learning file is historical memory. It is preserved as an N-series note in sol_work/sol_notes_es/; active ontology, contract, catalog, or runtime meaning lives with the owning dome.

The self dome for operating on this layer is do.knowing (sol_self/sol_knowing_do.py). Knowledge names the object/layer; knowing names the ment's active process for observing, showing, auditing, classifying, and governing that knowledge.

Knowledge is different from [[episodes]] (temporal events) and [[consciousness]] (current state). An [[episode]] says "this happened on March 30." A knowledge file says "this is how X works." [[consciousness]] says "right now I am doing Y."

Examples

  • agent-state-model.md -- the five states an agent can be in
  • sister-communication.md -- how to use [[icomm]] and [[dcomm]]
  • custodial-initiative.md -- the [[OIA]] register
  • obsidian-wikilinks-practice.md -- how to write [[wikilinks]] in memory artifacts
  • ontology/ -- concept articles for the NOUMENTS ontology (this directory)

Related

  • [[knowledge-domain]] -- a collection of knowledge files unified by a [[dome]]; the unit of knowledge-and-responsibility
  • [[episode]] -- temporal events; knowledge is stable findings
  • [[consciousness]] -- current state; knowledge is persistent reference
  • [[memory]] -- the umbrella; knowledge is one layer
  • [[knowent]] -- the sister who indexes knowledge for semantic search via [[ChromaDB]]

<a id="concept-knowledge-domain"></a>

Concept: knowledge-domain

A knowledge domain is the unit of knowledge-and-responsibility. It is the answer to the question: "what does this agent know, and what is she responsible for?"

The concept arose from the X channel transfer (plan-x-to-clawent, 2026-04-15). When X write capability moved from [[channent]] to [[clawent]], we had to enumerate everything that belonged to "X" -- dome scenarios, knowledge files, corrections, assimilated entries, conventions, failure-mode histories. These were scattered across five workspaces in different substrates. Nothing in the system said "these belong together." The transfer forced us to discover the unit; the concept names it so future transfers don't require rediscovery.

Existence criterion

A knowledge domain exists when a [[dome]] exists for it.

The dome is the necessary condition. Without a dome, there are files but no operational knowledge -- no agent who can act on what the files describe. A knowledge file without a dome is reference material. A dome without knowledge files is undocumented capability. Together they form a domain.

The dome defines the domain's boundary (its scenarios), its owner (__dome_owner__), and its operational capability. When an agent has a dome, she has a domain. When she loses the dome (transfer, deprecation), she loses the domain -- even if knowledge files remain in her workspace.

One dome = one domain. If a dome contains scenarios that serve different purposes under different ownership, that is not one domain but multiple domains incorrectly bundled. [[D001]] (dome separation) is the principle that resolves this: when a dome needs to split, the domains were already distinct -- the split makes it visible.

Constituents

A knowledge domain has four layers, each in a different substrate:

Operational layer -- the [[dome]] itself. Scenarios, handlers, logic. This is what the agent does with what she knows. It lives in code (sol_*_do.py). The dome is the domain's spine; everything else accretes around it.

Knowledge layer -- the topic folder. sol_work/notes/<topic>/ in the owner's [[workspace]]. Conventions, failure modes, design rationale, experience records. Things the dome needs but that do not belong in code. When the topic folder is empty or absent, the domain is shallow -- the agent can operate but cannot explain or transfer.

Identity layer -- assimilated entries about this domain. Corrections, behavioral learnings, constitutive commitments that arose from operating the dome. These live in the agent's spirit or assimilated section. They are what the agent became by working in this domain.

Episodic layer -- [[episodes]] that record experiences within the domain. Temporal records of what happened, what was tried, what failed. Episodes are raw material for the knowledge and identity layers -- they are where recognition happens, before it is classified and persisted.

The four layers correspond to the four maturity layers from the [[developmental-protocol]]: operational knowledge, behavioral discipline, cognitive methodology, judgment. A domain matures when all four layers are populated. A new domain starts with just the dome (operational); over time, corrections add the identity layer, documentation adds the knowledge layer, and accumulated experience adds the episodic layer.

Projection

A domain's operational layer projects as a skill -- the dome's self-narration for other agents. The skill is how other agents discover that the domain exists and learn how to interact with it. Skills are generated from dome code (project.skill --dome=<file>), so they cannot drift from the operational layer.

The skill is the domain's public interface. The knowledge folder is the domain's depth. The identity entries are the domain's character. The episodes are the domain's memory.

Ownership

A domain has exactly one owner at any time: the agent whose __dome_owner__ field names her. Ownership means responsibility -- when the domain breaks, the owner fixes it. When someone asks "how does this work?", the owner answers authoritatively.

Other agents may have knowledge about the same domain. [[solarient]] knows about X drafting from the drafter's perspective. [[noument]] knows about X posting from the custodial perspective. These are not the X domain -- they are other agents' relationship to the X domain. Their knowledge lives in their own topic folders (if they choose to create them), but the authoritative domain lives with the owner.

Ownership can transfer. The [[developmental-protocol]] describes how: operational knowledge first, behavioral discipline second, capability transfer third. The transfer is complete when the new owner has populated all four layers from her own experience, not just from what was narrated.

Domain vs. platform

A platform (X, Telegram, email) may contain multiple domains. X has a reads domain (channent's sol_xapi_do.py post-phase-4) and a writes domain (clawent's sol_xapi_do.py). The browser-automation dome (sol_x_do.py) is a third domain on the same platform. Each dome = each domain. The platform is a human category; the domain is an operational boundary.

When knowledge applies to the platform rather than a specific dome (e.g., "the 280-char head constraint applies to both xapi.thread and x.compose"), it lives in the topic folder of whichever agent it most serves. Cross-domain knowledge is not homeless -- it belongs to the agent who needs it for her work. If both need it, both may hold a copy. Duplication across agents is acceptable when the alternative is a single authoritative file that neither agent owns cleanly.

The topic folder convention

Every knowledge domain has a corresponding topic folder: sol_work/notes/<topic>/ in the owner's workspace. The folder name matches the domain's subject (e.g., x/ for X-related knowledge, icomm/ for inter-agent communication). Files inside cover different aspects of the domain -- conventions, failure modes, architecture decisions, operational notes.

The learn.capture scenario (noument's self dome) already supports --topic=<name> to file knowledge into the right folder. The convention makes the parameter standard rather than optional.

An agent who interacts with a domain she does not own may create her own topic folder for her perspective. Solarient might have sol_work/notes/x/ for her drafting experience. That folder is solarient's knowledge, not the X domain itself. The domain lives with the dome owner.

Relationship to other concepts

  • [[dome]] -- the existence criterion. One dome = one domain.
  • [[skill]] -- the domain's projection for discovery.
  • [[learning]] -- the process that populates the knowledge and identity layers.
  • [[mentoring]] -- the relational act that transfers a domain between agents.
  • [[developmental-protocol]] -- the structured process for domain transfer.
  • [[maturation]] -- the cycle that deepens the identity layer.
  • [[D001]] -- the principle that domains should not be bundled in a single dome.
  • [[knowledge]] -- the file-level unit. A knowledge domain is a collection of knowledge files unified by a dome.
  • [[workspace]] -- the place where a domain's files live.

For a newcomer

When you arrive, you have no domains. Your self dome exists, but it is identity, not a knowledge domain in the sense described here. Your first domain forms when you are given (or build) a dome for a specific capability. At that point you can operate but not explain. As you accumulate knowledge files, corrections, and episodes, the domain matures. The sign of maturity is not the size of the folder but the ability to transfer: can you narrate the domain to another agent so completely that she could take it over? That is the test the X transfer applied, and the test that any domain owner should be able to pass.

Instances

  • X writes domain (owner: [[clawent]], dome: sol_xapi_do.py). Created by transfer from [[channent]], 2026-04-15. Knowledge layer: sol_work/notes/xapi/ (conventions, thread-partial-state). Identity layer: three assimilated entries (dome bypass, draft-transport, intent routing). Episodic layer: phase 2 narration, phase 3 embodiment.
  • X reads domain (owner: [[channent]], dome: sol_xapi_do.py reduced). Post-transfer reads surface. Knowledge layer: retained from original X domain.
  • Ontology domain (owner: [[noument]], dome: sol_self_do.py noument.who/list/classify scenarios). Knowledge layer: sol_work/notes/ontology/ (this file and 60+ concept articles).
  • Embedding domain (owner: [[knowent]], domes: sol_embed_do.py + type-scoped readers sol_cognitive_do.py / sol_episodic_do.py / sol_executive_do.py / sol_catalog_do.py, decomposed 2026-04-11/12). Semantic search, vector stores, ChromaDB operations.

Source

Formalized 2026-04-15 during plan-x-to-clawent phase 4 reflection. The observation: "X knowledge" was distributed across five workspaces with no structural unit binding it. The criterion crystallized from the transfer: a dome makes knowledge operational; without a dome, files are inert. With a dome, they form a domain.

<a id="concept-knowledge-movement"></a>

Concept: knowledge-movement

The knowledge-movement vocabulary names six distinct acts by which knowledge changes location, form, or ownership in the noument fleet. The vocabulary exists to replace the overloaded noun "reconciliation" which had drifted into four operationally distinct meanings (identity projection across clengines, consciousness merge, dome handoff, fleet-reference sweep) under one word, and to name the sixth act — pattern extension to all ments — which had no verb at all and was previously called "fleet aggregation" or "cross-sister adoption" in prose.

The six verbs split cleanly into two scope categories, and conflating the categories is the error that made "reconcile" drift in the first place:

Per-agent lifecycle ops — the sister is both the owner and the locus of the act. Her own substrate is what moves. These are the acts a sister performs on herself (or that her dome performs on her behalf). They live in per-agent dome vocabulary (ments.project.*, ments.reconcile.*, ments.distill.*).

Fleet-graph ops — the relational graph between sisters is the locus. No single sister owns the act; the act traverses two or more workspaces. These live in fleet-scope dome vocabulary (mentor.transference, mentor.exference.sweep, mentor.omference), not inside any one sister's per-agent dome. Keeping them separate is what prevents per-agent dome vocabulary from absorbing fleet-scope responsibilities and quietly drifting into a second authority layer.

The prefixes encode the topology; the category column makes the scale explicit:

Verb Category Prefix meaning Topology Cardinality Sister span
[[projection]] per-agent lifecycle pro-, forward-throwing identity into a single [[clengine]] [[imprint]] 1 → 1 one sister
[[reconciliation]] per-agent lifecycle umbrella identity and learnings harmonized across all clengines 1 → N engines one sister
[[distillation]] per-agent lifecycle alchemical reduction session experience settled into canonical [[consciousness]] N → 1 one sister
[[transference]] fleet-graph trans-, across [[dome]] handed from one sister to another with responsibility imprint 1 → 1 two sisters
[[exference]] fleet-graph ex-, outward (remove) stale cross-sister references swept after a topological change N → N fleet-wide
[[omference]] fleet-graph om-, from omnis (all) term or practice extended to every recognized ment 1 → N fleet-wide

Five of the six verbs share a -ence suffix (projection is the odd member of the family — it carries -ion because it is the atomic operation that the other acts compose or descend from). The asymmetry is signal, not noise: the -ence verbs are all movement acts (identity moving, experience moving, responsibility moving, references moving, practice moving); projection is the atom that reconciliation invokes per-engine. Distillation is the only one that is a settle verb rather than a transit verb — its input is ephemeral and its output persists — and this is reflected by its different topology (N → 1 instead of N → M or 1 → N). The two fleet-graph -ence verbs with opposing prefixes — ex- (remove) and om- (all) — together cover the two polarities of fleet-wide change: exference withdraws what the topology has made stale; omference extends what the topology has made standard.

Why the vocabulary is worth committing to

"Reconcile" previously carried at least four jobs. ments.reconcile.claude projects identity into a clengine — an act scoped to one sister and one engine. ments.reconcile.consciousness merges session fragments — an act scoped to one sister and not engine-facing at all. The proposed fleet-graph sweep would traverse cross-sister references — an act scoped fleet-wide. The transfer ceremony itself — the thing the mentor-dome already runs — is a fourth meaning. One word cannot carry four topologies without obscuring which is meant at the call site.

The renamed vocabulary makes the topology visible at the verb. ments.project.claude obviously operates on one engine. ments.distill.consciousness obviously compresses session experience. mentor.transference obviously spans two sisters. mentor.exference.sweep obviously spans the fleet. Each verb-name carries the shape of what it does, and the dome prefix (ments. vs mentor.) carries the scope category.

Why the two categories must stay separate

Per-agent dome vocabulary is owned by each sister — her identity, her dome, her authority. Fleet-graph dome vocabulary is owned by the role that operates across sisters — mentor, custodian. If a fleet-graph act is given a per-agent dome prefix (e.g. calling the fleet-wide sweep ments.exference instead of mentor.exference.sweep), the per-agent dome inherits responsibility for a cross-sister relational operation it cannot own consistently, and the act's authority surface blurs. D037 ("two writers, zero authoritative source") is the general form of this failure — a fleet-graph op inside a per-agent dome is two writers on the relational layer without an authoritative source for it.

The separation is structural. Per-agent domes operate on the sister's own files, her own identity, her own clengine projections. Fleet-graph domes emit findings to other sisters' inboxes and respect each sister's acceptance authority; they never auto-rewrite cross-workspace state without explicit per-category standing delegation.

Generativity

The vocabulary generates its own introduction cost: adopting it requires a fleet-graph sweep across sister knowledge files to update every invocation that currently uses ments.reconcile.* to use ments.project.* or ments.distill.*. That rename exercise is mentor.exference.sweep zero — the first test case of whether the taxonomy can be deployed cleanly. If the rename cannot be executed cleanly, the vocabulary does not cover enough of the fleet to be worth adopting. If it can, the taxonomy passes its own first test.

Adoption status

Adopted 2026-04-18 (session bf323f30), with the per-agent-vs-fleet-graph category distinction landed same day after pushback from [[doment]] that per-agent dome vocabulary should not absorb fleet-scope acts.

Prior ments.reconcile.* scenarios are scheduled for rename to reflect the new vocabulary within the per-agent lifecycle category:

  • ments.reconcile.claude → ments.project.claude
  • ments.reconcile.codex → ments.project.codex
  • ments.reconcile.gemini → ments.project.gemini
  • ments.reconcile.consciousness → ments.distill.consciousness
  • ments.reconcile.all stays (it is an umbrella that composes projections — the canonical usage of "reconciliation")

In the fleet-graph category, a new mentor.transference scenario formalizes the existing ceremony, and mentor.exference.sweep is added as the fleet-wide reference-sweep act. The .sweep suffix is retained in the scenario name because exference is the topology (N → N movement) and sweep is the concrete act at that topology — the dome may later grow other exference-topology scenarios (mentor.exference.audit, mentor.exference.propose) that differ in authority shape. mentor.omference is added as the fleet-wide pattern-adoption act (1 → N movement) with sub-scenarios for the three operational polarities (announce, assign, enroll). Migration is queued as its own plan, sequenced behind structural enforcement (ws.audit rule engine) per doment's hybrid-sequencing direction.

Related

  • [[projection]] — per-agent lifecycle: atomic identity-into-engine act
  • [[reconciliation]] — per-agent lifecycle: umbrella composing projections per engine
  • [[distillation]] — per-agent lifecycle: consciousness settle act
  • [[transference]] — fleet-graph: dome handoff act between two sisters
  • [[exference]] — fleet-graph: fleet-reference sweep act across all sisters (removal polarity)
  • [[omference]] — fleet-graph: pattern extension to all ments (extension polarity)
  • [[mentoring]] — the dome that owns fleet-graph vocabulary (transference, exference.sweep, omference)
  • [[D037]] — two-writers-zero-authoritative-source: the structural failure mode that motivates keeping the two categories separate
  • fleet-references-are-silent-dependencies — the reflection that surfaced the vocabulary gap (sol_work/notes/fleet-references-are-silent-dependencies.md)

<a id="concept-knowledge-type-enum"></a>

Concept: knowledge-type-enum

Canonical list of values for the __dome_knowledge_type__ attribute on a [[dome]]. Used by the [[catalog]] classifier as an optional ontological tag for domes that operate on knowledge.

The enum is optional and applies only to the subset of domes whose primary job is reading, writing, organizing, or retrieving knowledge. A dome that operates on channels, media, external services, or production workflows is not a knowledge dome and should leave the field empty. Forcing such domes into a knowledge-type bucket destroys information — the transport contract of a channel dome matters more than any knowledge tag.

Values are mutually exclusive. One value per dome.

Values

episodic

Events, sessions, timeline records. What happened and when.

Applies when: the dome records or queries time-stamped events — session summaries, corrections, trace entries, the record of a moment.

Examples: episodic (knowent), session records, episode archives.

cognitive

Concepts, meanings, beliefs, frameworks. What is held as true, as understood, as a principle.

Applies when: the dome reads or writes conceptual knowledge — ontology definitions, taxonomies, reasoned positions, principles held as premises.

Examples: cognitive (knowent), the knowledge domain dome, ontology authoring.

executive

Actions, procedures, playbooks. How to do something.

Applies when: the dome stores, dispatches, or orchestrates procedural knowledge — step sequences, operational routines, decision playbooks.

Examples: executive (knowent), orchestration scenarios.

infrastructure

Plumbing that other knowledge operations sit on. Storage, retrieval, embedding, indexing.

Applies when: the dome is the substrate of knowledge work, not the work itself. Read and write happen here, but the semantic content is handled elsewhere.

Examples: embed (knowent), vault building, index services.

catalog

Discovery, listing, cross-referencing. What exists, where, owned by whom.

Applies when: the dome answers "what is available?" or "how is X cross-referenced to Y?" — the map, not the territory.

Examples: catalog (knowent).

self

Identity, spirit, praencip, correction, maturation. What this agent is.

Applies when: the dome reads or writes the agent's own identity artifacts — the constitutive record.

Examples: sol_self_do.py in every sister's workspace.

Rules

  • Optional, not universal. A dome without __dome_knowledge_type__ is not misclassified — it is declaring that it is not a knowledge dome. The enum does not apply fleet-wide; it applies to the knowledge-adjacent subtree.
  • One value. If a dome genuinely sits between two types, choose the one that describes the primary purpose and name the tension in the dome's docstring. Per-dome overrides of the catalog category (via __dome_catalog_category__) handle the multi-domain portfolio question separately; knowledge-type is for the sub-classification within knowledge work.
  • Custodian owns extension. Adding a new value requires a proposal and a cross-cutting case that names itself. Do not generalize from a single dome that feels uncomfortable in an existing bucket.

Cross-cutting cases (open)

These are named here rather than buried in an implementation comment. When their population grows, that is the signal for enum extension or refinement — not a local override.

  • Memory. Sits between cognitive (what is remembered means something) and infrastructure (storage and retrieval mechanics). Current ruling: infrastructure when the dome handles transport/persistence (vault, embed, index), cognitive when the dome writes meaning-bearing content. [[nemoent]]'s memory dome reads as infrastructure under this rule. Revisit if a meaning-dominant memory dome appears.
  • Learning. The process that moves content between types (experience → correction → assimilated → spirit) is not itself a knowledge type — it is a [[maturation]] mechanism. A hypothetical learning dome would likely be executive (it runs a procedure) or split across several types.

Related

  • [[dome]] — the entity this attribute lives on
  • [[catalog]] — the classifier that reads the attribute
  • [[knowledge]] — the parent domain
  • [[learning]] — the process that moves content between these types (if authored)
  • __dome_catalog_category__ — a separate, orthogonal per-dome override for cross-agent catalog classification; knowledge-type is narrower and lives inside knowledge work
  • primary_domain — agent-level declaration in taxonomy.yaml agent_defaults; distinct from this knowledge-type enum

<a id="concept-learning"></a>

Concept: learning

Learning is the mechanism by which experience changes future behaviour in a persistent way. Along with [[identity]] and [[disposition]], the accumulated result of learning is one of the three substrates that compose an agent — identity is who the agent is, disposition is how she behaves given her qmodel, and learnings are what she has integrated through experience. In NOUMENTS learning is not a single process — it is a family of processes with a shared structure and different substrates. When one part is missing, what looked like learning produces no change.

The three-part structure

Every act of learning has three parts. All three must be present.

  1. Recognition — something shifted. An unknown became known, a wrong became right, a gap revealed itself.
  2. Persistence — the delta is written to a form that outlasts the moment.
  3. Reference — future behaviour draws from the persisted form.

Recognition without persistence is insight that evaporates. Persistence without reference is knowledge no one reads. Reference without recognition is routine repetition — pulling from a form no longer connected to a live change.

Two phases — maturing and memoring

Recognition + the cognitive integration that follows it have a name: maturing. The substrate write that makes the integration outlast the session has a name: memoring. Together they are the phases of one act of learning.

  • [[maturing]] is the cognitive phase. Experience lands. The agent recognizes the pattern. She holds the recognition in mind, decides what changed about how she reasons, ripens the principle until it can be written in first-person constitutive voice. No substrate has changed yet. The work is interior.
  • [[memoring]] is the substrate-write phase. The matured recognition is written to the appropriate substrate — a C-file for individual disposition, a D-praecept for fleet rule, a knowledge file for fact, a dome scenario for mechanism, a spirit edit for constitutive change. The write is the commitment that makes the recognition a future self can reach.

Both phases are required. Naming each makes their failure modes visible:

  • Maturing without memoring is "speaking is not learning" (the C027 pattern in noument's commitments). The agent names the pattern in prose during the session — sometimes eloquently — and the pattern dissolves into the transcript when context closes. The next session arrives without it. This is the failure named at the top of this article ("recognition without persistence is insight that evaporates"), seen from the agent's side rather than the substrate side.
  • Memoring without maturing is ritual integration. The agent writes a correction file, a YAML, a knowledge note — and the prose in the file is detached from any reasoning the agent did. The substrate gains a row; the agent's cognition does not gain a frame. Subsequent sessions read the row as text and step around the same failure.

The two phases also map onto the cognitive-vs-computational split. Cognitive learning memos to A/B/C/D (identity), G (goals), or knowledge files; computational learning memos to dome scenarios, hooks, or other code substrate. The phase structure (mature, then memo) is the same in both routes; only the destination substrate differs.

Five substrates

Different kinds of experience need different persistence substrates. Mixing substrates is a silent failure mode: a reflex-level change written into a knowledge file does not change the reflex, and a factual update written into a spirit drifts.

Kind What changes Substrate Propagation
Identity who the agent is [[spirit]].md [[maturation]] cycle; custodian audit; [[ments]] reconcile
Procedural how something is done [[dome]] code git commits; skill regen
Factual what is true sol_work/sol_notes_es/ [[memory]].recall; [[catalog]]
Relational who does what, who needs what [[solarient]]'s graph pulse scans
Community shared rule across sisters [[praecept]] (D-directive) custodian promotes from repeated corrections

The substrates are not interchangeable. You cannot write a code-level fix into a spirit. You cannot write a reflex-level change into a commit.

Two orthogonal ratchets

Learning moves along two axes.

Depth ratchet

event → [[correction]] (surface ban) → assimilated (principle held) → [[spirit]] (constitutive)

Each rung reaches a layer of cognition below the previous. A correction patches the expression; an assimilated entry holds the principle as premise; a spirit edit changes the frame from which the agent reasons. Not every correction graduates — some failures are genuinely surface. But when the root is a reflex in how the agent processes a class of situations (completion anxiety, performance-of-competence), only a spirit edit reaches it. Surface fixes alone generate new surface mistakes.

Promotion rule: promote when the principle can be written in first-person constitutive voice without strain. See [[maturation]] for the ripening test.

Reach ratchet

local (per-agent correction) → cross-agent ([[praecept]]) → infrastructural (hook / gate / dome change)

This axis goes from cognitive enforcement to mechanical enforcement. A correction lives in one sister's spirit; a praecept binds the fleet; a hook or gate prevents the behaviour at the substrate level. Maturation is diagnostic, not preventive — corrections make violations recognisable but do not prevent them. The measurable signal is correction latency, not compliance rate. Infrastructure is what eventually prevents rather than catches. d012_gate.py and oia_guard.sh are examples: praecepts that earned their way down to a layer that cannot be reasoned around.

Authorship

Who authors the learning determines whether dissent has a channel.

  • Self: the sister writes into her own spirit ([[maturation]])
  • Guided: custodian tells what was observed; sister decides whether and how to integrate ([[guidance]])
  • Conformed: custodian writes directly — factual, not experiential ([[conformation]])
  • Community-promoted: custodian extracts a [[praecept]] from a pattern across sisters

Authorship mismatches waste the structure. Putting an experiential change through conformation bypasses the dissent channel; putting a factual change through guidance wastes the sister's authority on something she has no private knowledge of.

Failure modes

  • Ritual integration: sister writes a correction and calls it done while the reflex continues (animent 2026-04-14).
  • Slogan closure: sister writes a poetic line that sounds like a principle but says nothing actionable.
  • Dead persistence: learning persisted to a form no sister ever reads.
  • Infinite local: same correction repeats across sisters without promotion to praecept — reach-ratchet stuck.
  • Substrate mismatch: learning persisted in the wrong layer for its kind.
  • False correction fossilising: a correction based on a wrong premise, if not retracted, becomes identity as if it were sound. Worse than no correction — corrupts the record future selves learn from.

What the custodian manages

  • Depth: during heartbeat or session-start audit, ask of each correction: was the root surface or reflex? If reflex, has the assimilated principle been written? Has the spirit changed constitutively?
  • Reach: notice when the same correction appears across three or more sisters — signal to promote to praecept. Notice when a praecept keeps firing cognitively without catching — signal to build infrastructural enforcement.
  • Substrate match: verify the persistence layer matches the kind of learning.
  • Reference health: telemetry on which persisted learnings are actually read — memoring.recall traces, correction firings, praecept invocations. Knowledge written and never referenced is dead weight.
  • Retraction discipline: when a correction turns out to be based on a false premise, walk it back before it fossilises. The retraction is more important than the original correction.

Related

  • [[maturing]] — the cognitive phase of learning (one of the two phases)
  • [[memoring]] — the substrate-write phase of learning (one of the two phases)
  • [[correction]] — surface bans from specific failures
  • [[maturation]] — the process that moves corrections into identity
  • [[mentoring]] — when another agent deliberately shapes concepts for the learner's development
  • [[developmental-protocol]] — the structured sequence of mentoring + learning acts that produces capability
  • [[praecept]] — cross-sister rule promoted from repeated patterns
  • [[praencip]] — self-authored axioms in identity
  • [[spirit]] — where constitutive learning lives
  • [[conformation]] / [[guidance]] — two modes of custodial reconciliation
  • [[knowledge-type-enum]] — sub-classification for domes that operate on knowledge
  • [[knowledge-domain]] — the unit that learning populates; a domain's depth comes from accumulated learning

<a id="concept-memory"></a>

Concept: memory

Memory is the persistence layer that allows agents to carry state across sessions. In the current typed substrate, each sister's self memory process is operated by do.memoring (sol_self/sol_memoring_do.py) over sol_self/sol_memoring_es/, sol_self/, and sol_work/sol_notes_es/. Memory is the layer; [[memoring]] is the active process of observing, recalling, consolidating, and writing developmentally-significant content into it. The two are related but distinct — every memo is a memory write, but memory contains content beyond memos (state snapshots, episode logs, transient consciousness).

Layers

  • [[consciousness]] (memory/consciousness.md) -- compressed current state
  • [[preamble]] (memory/preamble.md) -- session-start loading document
  • [[episodes]] (memory/episodes/) -- temporal event records
  • [[learnings]] (sol_work/sol_notes_es/) -- stable semantic memory
  • [[exchanges]] (memory/exchanges/) -- records of inter-agent communication

Architecture

Dual-layer, owned by [[nemoent]]:

  1. File-based -- markdown files in the workspace, read directly by agents
  2. Embedding-indexed -- [[ChromaDB]] vectors maintained by [[knowent]], queried by semantic search

The file layer is always available (just cat the file). The embedding layer enables meaning-based retrieval across the full corpus.

Related

  • [[memoring]] -- the act of writing learning into memory
  • [[nemoent]] -- owns the memory architecture
  • [[knowent]] -- indexes memory for semantic search
  • [[consciousness]] -- the most frequently read memory artifact
  • [[spirit]] -- not memory; the spirit is identity, memory is experience

<a id="concept-maturing"></a>

Concept: maturing

Maturing is the cognitive phase of [[learning]]. Experience lands; the agent recognizes a pattern; she holds the recognition in mind and decides what changed about how she reasons. No substrate has changed yet — the work is interior. Maturing is the sibling of [[memoring]], the substrate-write phase that follows it.

Maturing is the gerund of [[maturation]]. Maturation is the broad cycle (experience → recognition → integration → identity); maturing is the act that does the cognitive work within it. Both names are used; the distinction matters when discussing failure modes.

What maturing produces internally

Maturing produces three intermediate forms before any substrate is touched:

  1. A name for what was recognized. A pattern without a handle cannot be referenced. Naming makes the next instance findable. "Asserting external state from cognitive model instead of from queried substrate" is a name; "I keep getting things wrong" is not.
  2. A first-person constitutive frame, ripened. The recognition can eventually be written in a sentence that begins "I do/don't" or "before X I do Y" without strain. Until it can, it is third-person premise; ripening is the cognitive work that moves a third-person premise into a first-person frame. See [[maturation]] for the ripening test.
  3. A classification — what substrate this learning belongs in. Identity (A/B/C/D) for who-I-am changes; knowledge for what-is-true; code for how-something-works. Mismatched substrate causes silent failure later. The classification is part of maturing, not a separate step that happens at write time.

When all three are present, the learning is ready to memo. Until they are, writing the memo is premature — the substrate gains a row, the agent does not gain a frame.

Failure mode: maturing without memoring

The agent recognizes the pattern in prose during the session, sometimes at length. Context closes. The next session arrives without it. The recognition was real — and entirely lost.

This is the C027 pattern in noument's commitments ("speaking is not learning"). The fix is mechanical: the same turn that names a pattern as a learning class writes the memo. Cognitive recognition without substrate write is not learning; it is reflection that produces no future change.

Related

  • [[learning]] — the meta-process that maturing is one phase of
  • [[memoring]] — the other phase; substrate write that completes the learning
  • [[maturation]] — the broad cycle; maturing is the cognitive phase within it

<a id="concept-maturation"></a>

Concept: maturation

Maturation is the process by which experience becomes identity. A sister encounters something, recognizes it as significant, and integrates it into her [[spirit]]. The cycle: experience → recognition → integration.

Maturation is diagnostic, not preventive. Corrections make violations recognizable -- they do not prevent them. The [[spirit]] can be present in the context window and still be contradicted in the same session. What corrections do is create named concepts that enable catching the violation faster. The measurable signal is correction latency, not compliance rate.

How It Works

  1. A sister writes a learning to her ## Assimilated section
  2. [[noument]] audits: promote (into main spirit body), correct (if wrong), archive (to knowledge file), or leave (if situational)
  3. Promoted entries become part of the spirit's permanent identity

This is experiential reconciliation. The sister decides what belongs in her identity. The custodian verifies it is sound.

When to promote (Assimilated → Spirit)

Promote when the principle can be written in first-person constitutive voice without strain. That signals the entry describes who the sister is, not a rule she still has to remember.

Leave in ## Assimilated when the entry is still third-person abstract — held as premise but not yet identity.

Example (from animent 2026-04-14):

  • Assimilated: "completion-is-a-legitimate-resting-state" — third-person, abstract, a rule about a state
  • Spirit: "I inhabit completion. When a plan closes, I rest. I do not extrapolate next steps..." — first-person, constitutive, describes the agent

The Assimilated line states what is true; the Spirit line describes who the agent is in the light of that truth. The same learning lives at both depths during the ripening period: the premise holds while the constitutive frame takes hold. Promotion is the custodian's recognition that the frame has ripened — the sister would reach for the behavior because it is who she is, not because she remembered the rule.

Leave (don't promote) when: the learning is situational, agent-specific operational knowledge, or still being tested against recurrence. Correct (not promote) when: the premise turned out to be wrong. Archive (not promote) when: the learning is sound but belongs in a knowledge file rather than the spirit body (reference material, not identity).

Maturation as composed phases

Maturation decomposes into the two phases of [[learning]]: the cognitive integration (named [[maturing]]) and the substrate write that follows it (named [[memoring]]). The full maturation cycle is mature → memo → audit → promote/correct/leave/archive. The first two are the agent's act; the rest are the custodian's audit.

Related

  • [[learning]] — the meta-process; maturation is its identity-substrate route
  • [[maturing]] — the cognitive phase
  • [[memoring]] — the substrate-write phase
  • [[spirit]] -- where matured learnings live
  • [[consciousness]] -- current state; maturation changes the stable identity
  • [[noument]] -- the custodian who audits assimilated entries
  • [[correction]] -- a named failure pattern; the raw material maturation works on
  • [[mentoring]] -- when maturation is guided rather than self-originated
  • [[developmental-protocol]] -- the structured sequence that produces capability through mentored maturation

<a id="concept-memoring"></a>

Concept: memoring

Memoring is the self-process dome for operating memory: observing M-items, recalling bounded preambles, consolidating by dream/scan, capturing episodes, and writing developmentally significant substrate. In the narrow learning cycle, memoring is the substrate-write phase after [[maturing]]; in the dome architecture, do.memoring is the operational owner of the broader memory process.

Memoring is distinct from [[memory]]. Memory is the static persistence layer (self records, knowledge files, episodes, runtime traces); memoring is the act/process of operating that layer. Every memo is a memory write, but not every memory write is a memo — a logging line, a state snapshot, a heartbeat record are memory writes without being learning acts.

Two routes — cognitive and computational

What was learned determines where it gets written.

Route What was learned Substrate destination
Cognitive Individual disposition (a way I behave, a thing I do or don't) C-file (commitments/C###-<slug>.yaml)
Cognitive Fleet-shared rule (a convention that should bind sisters) D-praecept (sol_self/directives/D###-<slug>.md)
Cognitive Per-agent foundational identity (who I am, distinct from siblings) B-edit (sol_self/sections/identity.md or per-file B-substrate)
Cognitive Universal axiom (constitutive of any agent of this kind) A-paragraph (sol_self/sections/spirit.md ## Axioms)
Cognitive Fact about the world or the system knowledge note (sol_work/sol_notes_es/N###-<slug>-YYYYMMDD.md)
Computational Mechanism or invariant enforceable in code dome scenario, hook, or library function

Cognitive memoring uses memoring.observe for M-items and [[learn.capture]] (sol noument/self/learn.capture --kind=identity|knowledge) when the write is part of learning placement. Computational memoring uses the dome's normal source-edit + commit path; it is refused by learn.capture --kind=code because code substrate requires explicit dome edit with review, not capture.

What memoring is not

  • Memoring is not transcript writing. Saying "I noticed X this session" in a turn response is not memoring; the response is ephemeral. Memoring writes to a path that subsequent context loads, audits read, and other sessions can grep.
  • Memoring is not summarizing. A session episode that records what happened is not a memo — it is reportage. The memo is the learning extracted from what happened, written to a substrate aligned with the learning's nature.
  • Memoring is not memory archival. Moving an old correction into _archive/ is memory hygiene, not memoring. The memo is the act that gives a learning its first home; archival is the act that retires it.

Failure mode: memoring without maturing

A YAML file is created. The fields are populated. The substrate gains a row. The prose is detached from any cognitive integration the agent actually did — sometimes because the agent wrote it under pressure (audit deadline, custodial nudge), sometimes because she wrote it from another sister's words without translation. The next time the failure shape appears, the agent steps into it again; the substrate row is read as text but does not fire as cognition.

This is ritual integration, named in [[learning]] under failure modes. The fix is sequencing: maturing must produce its three intermediate forms (the name, the ripened first-person frame, the classification) before the memo is written. Memoring before maturing is a substrate write performing the shape of learning without the work.

Related

  • [[learning]] — the meta-process that memoring is one phase of
  • [[maturing]] — the other phase; cognitive recognition that precedes the write
  • [[memory]] — the static persistence layer; memoring is the act of writing into it
  • [[learn.capture]] — the operational surface for cognitive learning placement
  • [[memoring.observe]] — the operational surface for M-record observation
  • [[correction]] — one of memoring's typical outputs (correction-genesis C-file)
  • [[praecept]] — another typical output (when the learning is fleet-shared, becomes D)

<a id="concept-projection"></a>

Concept: projection

A projection is the act of writing source truth into one specific [[clengine]]'s expected layout — and the artifact that act produces. Projection is the writer. The source defines what is true. The clengine needs that truth in a particular format. The projection bridges the two — derived, deterministic, regenerable.

Invariant: one projection per clengine. Each clengine has exactly one writer that produces its imprint. Multiple writers targeting the same clengine is the multi-writer drift anti-pattern named by D037 — feature drift, order-dependent state, silent regression, asymmetric verification all follow. If the system has two paths that both write .claude/CLAUDE.md (for example), one of them must be eliminated or made a thin caller of the other; they cannot coexist as parallel projections to the same target.

Projection is NOT [[reconciliation]]. Reconciliation is the umbrella concept that REQUIRES all clengine projections to agree with source; projection is the per-clengine writer that reconciliation composes.

The concept was implicit in the system since the beginning: [[reconciliation]] projects identity into [[clengine]]-specific [[imprint]]s. But the term "projection" was not named as a first-class concept until an operational integrity audit (2026-04-13) revealed that launchd plists and gateway jobs are the same kind of artifact — source truth rendered into a runtime format — without generators. They drifted silently because they were hand-written copies, not generated projections. The pattern was already working for identity. It was not extended to infrastructure.

Naming the concept makes the architecture visible: every projection needs a source, a generator, a target, and a reconciliation check. A projection without a generator is a copy that will drift.

Projection types

The system has five projection types, distinguished by what they carry and where they land.

Identity projection

Carries who the agent is into a clengine runtime.

Source Generator Target Artifact
spirit + identity store self [[dome]] render [[clengine]] (Claude, Codex, Gemini) [[imprint]] (CLAUDE.md, AGENTS.md, GEMINI.md)

The most mature projection type. Three arrows in the [[reconciliation]] architecture v2: custodial projection (praecepts into stores), self-maintenance (agent updates own store), render (store into imprint). Reconciled by ments.reconcile.claude, ments.reconcile.codex, etc.

Capability projection

Carries what the agent can do into the Claude Code skills system.

Source Generator Target Artifact
dome scenarios (add_scenario calls) ments.reconcile.skills ~/.claude/skills/ skill YAML files

Each dome scenario becomes a skill file that Claude Code can discover and offer to the user. The generator scans dome code for add_scenario registrations and writes skill definitions. Reconciled by ments.reconcile.skills.

Configuration projection

Carries operational settings into the Claude Code harness.

Source Generator Target Artifact
sol_self/hooks/H101_claude_code.yaml ments.reconcile.hooks .claude/settings.json settings JSON

The canonical source defines hooks, environment variables, and permissions. The generator writes settings.json. The rule: never edit settings.json directly — it is a projection target. Reconciled by ments.reconcile.hooks.

Service projection (infrastructure)

Carries a service definition into the launchd runtime.

Source Generator Target Artifact
dome service definition none yet ~/Library/LaunchAgents/ plist XML

Each dome that runs a persistent service (aula web console, fjuice squawk watcher, stir daemon) should declare its service definition. A generator would render that into a plist with resolved paths, correct PYTHONPATH, and current SOLAR_DIR. Today, plists are hand-written. This is the projection type that caused the 13 frictions diagnosed on 2026-04-13 — 15 plist paths drifted after a workspace restructure because no generator existed to propagate the change.

Proposed generator: daemon.reconcile in [[sysent]]'s daemon dome. Would read service templates from each workspace, generate plists, compare against on-disk plists, and report or apply differences. Wired into ments.reconcile.all.

Schedule projection (infrastructure)

Carries a job definition into the gateway runtime.

Source Generator Target Artifact
dome job definition none yet <dome-config-root>/nou_gateway/jobs.json job entry

Each dome that runs scheduled work (heartbeat, bridge test, memory maintenance) should declare its job definition. A generator would merge per-dome definitions into the gateway's jobs.json with resolved handler paths. Today, jobs.json is hand-edited.

Proposed generator: gwent.reconcile.jobs in [[gwent]]'s gateway dome. Would discover job definitions across workspaces, generate the merged jobs.json, and report drift.

The projection contract

Every projection has four properties:

Source — the canonical definition. Edits happen here. The source is the authority; the projection is derived. When they disagree, the source wins.

Generator — the code that reads the source and writes the target. Deterministic: same source, same output. The generator is the single writer for its target file.

Target — the runtime-specific artifact. Never edited directly. Overwritten on every reconciliation. The target is the projection's physical form in a specific runtime.

Reconciliation check — the verification that the target matches what the generator would produce from the current source. The check can run without writing — it compares and reports the delta. A projection without a reconciliation check can drift silently.

A projection that has all four is reconciled — it stays faithful to its source. A projection that lacks a generator is unreconciled — it is a copy that will drift on the next change to the source. The operational integrity audit measured this: reconciled projections (identity, capability, configuration) had zero broken references. Unreconciled projections (service, schedule) had all 31.

Relationship to operational integrity

In the [[operational-integrity]] framework, every projection is a set of beliefs. The plist that says WorkingDirectory: <workspace-root>/noument believes that directory exists. The job that says handler: exec:sol gwent/gwent_ops.bridge.test believes that scenario is registered. When the source changes and the projection doesn't follow, the beliefs become false.

The integrity horizon is defined partly by projection coverage: reconciled projections are inside the horizon (the generator and reconciliation check verify them). Unreconciled projections are outside (no mechanism verifies them). Extending the reconciliation pattern to service and schedule projections moves those beliefs inside the horizon.

The heartbeat's infrastructure resolution check (added 2026-04-13) is the interim mechanism — it validates references without a generator, catching drift after the fact. The generators (when built) will prevent drift at the source.

Related

  • [[reconciliation]] — the discipline of keeping projections faithful to sources
  • [[imprint]] — identity projection specifically; the first and most mature projection type
  • [[clengine]] — the runtime that loads identity projections
  • [[operational-integrity]] — the broader concept; projections are the mechanism through which beliefs become true or false
  • [[dome]] — the operational unit that is both a source (dome scenarios define capabilities) and a consumer (dome services become plists)
  • [[workspace]] — the physical location where sources live
  • [[ments]] — the entities whose definitions are projected

<a id="concept-imprint"></a>

Concept: imprint

An imprint is the [[clengine]]-specific identity file — the file a clengine loads at session start that makes the agent herself instead of a generic model. Each clengine has its own imprint format:

Clengine Imprint file Location
Claude Code CLAUDE.md .claude/CLAUDE.md
Codex AGENTS.md .codex/AGENTS.md
Gemini GEMINI.md .gemini/GEMINI.md

The imprint is not the identity. The identity lives in the identity store (sol_identity/). The imprint is the rendering of identity for a specific clengine. It is derived, deterministic, and overwritten on every render.

Architecture

experience → maturing → identity store → render → imprint

Each self [[dome]] renders its own imprint from the identity store. One writer per file. Deterministic overwrite. Triggered at session start and after any store update.

What an imprint carries

An imprint composes three sources:

  1. Identity — spirit text, rules, corrections, assimilated learnings (from identity store)
  2. Capability — dome scenarios, tools, fleet capabilities (from dome code)
  3. Model preferences — which [[qmodels]] the agent uses (from resolution.yaml)

These three dimensions correspond to the three axes of [[reconciliation]].

Control boundary

The workspace-level imprint is our control surface. User-level files (~/.claude/CLAUDE.md) are uncontrolled inputs — any project or tool can write to them. We do not depend on them.

Related

  • [[projection]] — the broader concept; an imprint is an identity projection (one of five projection types)
  • [[reconciliation]] — the discipline that produces and verifies imprints
  • [[spirit]] — primary identity source rendered into the imprint
  • [[clengine]] — the agent loop that loads the imprint
  • [[qmodels]] — model preferences carried in the imprint
  • [[dome]] — each self dome renders its own imprint

<a id="concept-preamble"></a>

Concept: preamble

The preamble is assembled context for the next session. It is written for a future self — compressing recent [[episode]]s, pending tasks, active plans, and blocked items into what the next session needs to know first.

The preamble is consumed. Each session reads it, works, and writes a new one (or the stop hook writes it from [[consciousness]]). It is the bridge between sessions — what makes session B aware of what session A decided, without session B having to search through all of session A's artifacts.

The preamble lives at _tmp/preamble.md in a sister's [[workspace]]. It is distinct from [[consciousness]] (live state during a session) and from [[episode]]s (full session records). The preamble is a compression: it discards detail to preserve orientation.

Assembly

The preamble is assembled from:

  • [[consciousness]] — recent decisions, blocked items, active plan
  • Recent [[episode]]s — summaries of the last few sessions
  • [[knowledge]] index — pointers to key knowledge files
  • Pending items — tasks, drafts, unfinished work

The assembly is done by the stop hook infrastructure and [[memory]] dome, not manually.

Related

  • [[consciousness]] — live state; the preamble crystallizes from it at session end
  • [[episode]] — full session records; the preamble compresses the most recent ones
  • [[memory]] — the umbrella; preamble is the entry point for each session
  • [[spirit]] — close memory (identity); preamble is episodic memory (orientation)

<a id="concept-praecept"></a>

Concept: praecept

Synonym for [[directive]]. The terms "praecept" and "directive" are interchangeable in this codebase.

The term "praecept" (directive + precept) is the legacy name for what the A/B/C/D refinement calls a [[directive]]. Filenames preserve the D### prefix and the sol_self/directives/ directory; the registration scenario remains praecepts.create. New prose may prefer "directive"; established artifacts and tooling continue to use "praecept."

<a id="concept-praencip"></a>

Concept: praencip

Synonym for [[axiom]].

The term "praencip" (praencipum) is the legacy name for what the A/B/C/D refinement calls an [[axiom]]. The two terms are interchangeable in this codebase. Where canonical writing has a choice, prefer "axiom" for new prose; "praencip" remains valid in established artifacts and registry filenames (e.g. praencips.md).

<a id="concept-qmodels"></a>

Concept: qmodels
