Transference is the act by which responsibility for a [[dome]] passes from one sister to another. The dome's files are not merely moved — ownership, practice, behavioral imprint, forwarding stubs, skills regeneration, and spirit updates all flow together in a single ceremony. The [[mentoring]] dome runs the ceremony across a structured state machine (planned → assessed → moved → projected → imprinted → audited → smoked → complete).

The prefix trans- (across) carries the topology: the operation spans two sisters — source A and destination B — and what crosses between them is not just a file but the whole set of responsibilities attached to the dome. Transference is 1 → 1 across two sisters in the [[knowledge-movement]] taxonomy.

The word is chosen deliberately over "handoff" or "transfer." Handoff is too casual for what actually happens — it suggests passing a baton, whereas transference involves imprinting behavioral expectations onto the new owner, preserving compatibility for existing callers via forwarding stubs, and regenerating the derived artifacts (skills, hooks, registry entries) that depend on the dome's location. The clinical/psychological resonance of "transference" — projecting relational expectations onto a new figure — is apt: the mentor-dome does effectively transfer the community's practice-relation with the dome from A to B.

Operation

Input Operator Output Dome
dome D currently owned by sister A ceremony across eight states dome D owned by sister B with preserved caller compatibility and regenerated derivatives mentor.plan, mentor.assess, mentor.move, mentor.project, mentor.imprint, mentor.audit, mentor.smoke, mentor.status

The fifth-dimension gap

As specified before 2026-04-18, transference has four dimensions: source spirit update, destination spirit update, skills regeneration, forwarding stub installation. The channent → [[clawent]] X-dome transference on 2026-04-15 revealed that fleet-wide knowledge references form a fifth dimension the ceremony did not address — 30 stale references across 11 files in 5 other sisters' workspaces drifted invisibly for three days. The proposed fix adds [[exference]] as a fifth dimension to the ceremony, so every transference automatically includes a fleet-reference sweep.

Related

  • [[exference]] — the proposed fifth dimension; every transference should trigger an exference
  • [[knowledge-movement]] — the taxonomy that names transference alongside projection, reconciliation, distillation, and exference
  • [[mentoring]] — the dome that runs the ceremony
  • [[conformation]] — structural identity updates that happen inside the imprint phase
  • [[guidance]] — experiential identity updates that happen inside the imprint phase

<a id="concept-service"></a>

Concept: service

A service is a named capability offered by a ment to the collective, accessible through a [[dome]] [[scenario]], with a defined contract and consent model. Services are the operational interface between ments — the answer to "what can she do for me, and under what terms?"

What a service is

A service binds four things:

  1. A capability — what the service does. "Publish a post to X." "Search collective memory by meaning." "Generate an image from a prompt."
  2. An owner — which ment provides it. [[Clawent]] owns X publishing (moved from channent 2026-04-15). [[Knowent]] owns semantic search. [[Dalent]] owns image generation.
  3. An interface — the dome scenario through which the service is invoked. sol clawent/x.post, sol knowent/vault.search, sol dalent/img.generate. The scenario defines the parameters, the return type, and the execution contract.
  4. A consent model — under what terms the service may be used. Most services are pre-authorized: by publishing the dome scenario, the owner consents to any ment calling it. Some services require per-transaction consent (the owner can refuse, negotiate, or redirect).

What a service is not

A self dome scenario is not a service. noument.who is noument's identity expressed as code — only noument runs it, and it answers questions about noument's domain (the registry). It is an identity operation, not a service offered to others. The test from the [[dome]] taxonomy: "Can this scenario run with --agent=musician in a workspace it has never seen?" If yes, it is a service (domain dome). If no, it is identity (self dome).

A shared infrastructure artifact is not a service. noumentlib.py is a library, not a dome. It has no owner in the service sense — it belongs to the commons. Services have owners; infrastructure has maintainers.

A raw file is not a service. A sister's knowledge files are readable by anyone, but reading them is not invoking a service — there is no interface, no contract, no consent mechanism. The dome-first principle exists to route access through the service interface rather than around it.

Service catalog

Every ment's service catalog is the set of domain dome scenarios she publishes. The catalog is:

  • Declared in the dome source code (each handler method is a scenario)
  • Discovered by [[knowent]]'s vault (vault.refresh scans all workspaces for dome files)
  • Addressed through capability addressing ([[address]] Layer 5: clawent/x.post resolves the scenario name to the dome file and invokes it)
  • Documented through dome-generated skills (project.skill --dome=<file>)

The catalog is not declared in the spirit or in agent.yaml — it is generated from the code. This is the dome-as-source principle: the dome is authoritative, everything else is projection.

Resource-to-service mapping

Each [[resource-sharing]] mode relates to services differently:

Sharing mode Service relationship
Dome invocation Direct service call. The caller invokes a published scenario.
Consultation Query beyond the service interface. The caller asks the owner something the dome does not expose as a scenario.
Co-construction Two service owners reason together outside either's catalog. The output may become a new service.
Delegation Service request with autonomy. The caller specifies intent; the delegate chooses which of her services to use.
Semantic query Infrastructure service. Knowent's search is a service; the memories searched are not.
Direct file read Bypasses the service interface. Accesses raw resources the owner has not published as a service.
Shared infrastructure Uses commons, not services. No owner, no service contract.
Conformation Custodial write. Not a service — the custodian acts on structural authority, not on a published interface.
Guidance Custodial observation. Not a service — the custodian offers, the sister decides. No dome scenario mediates.
Offloading Misrouted service request. The capability exists as a general tool, not as the recipient's service.

The mapping reveals a pattern: modes 1, 4, and 5 are service-mediated (they go through dome scenarios). Mode 2 is consultation that indicates a gap in the service interface — if the caller needs to ask, the dome might be missing a scenario. Mode 6 bypasses services entirely. Modes 8 and 9 operate outside the service model (custodial authority). Mode 3 produces candidates for new services. Mode 10 is a service request sent to the wrong provider.

Service lifecycle

Services are not static. They emerge, mature, and sometimes retire:

  1. Gap recognition. A sister asks another sister a question that the dome cannot answer (consultation). Or a caller bypasses the dome to read raw files (direct file read). Both signal a gap in the service catalog.
  2. Service creation. The dome owner adds a new scenario. The scenario defines parameters, return type, and behavior. The service is immediately available.
  3. Discovery. Knowent's vault indexes the new scenario. Other sisters can find it through vault.sisters or vault.search.
  4. Adoption. Sisters begin calling the service. The pre-authorized consent model means no per-use negotiation is needed.
  5. Maturation. Usage reveals edge cases. The owner refines the scenario. The service improves through use.
  6. Extraction. If a self dome scenario turns out to be a general capability, it is extracted into a domain dome — moving from identity to service. The dome-audit process identifies these candidates.

Service contract

A service contract is implicit in the dome scenario's interface:

  • Parameters: what the caller must provide (--agent, --prompt, --draft_file)
  • Return: what the caller receives (structured output, side effects, or both)
  • Idempotency: whether calling the service twice produces the same result
  • Side effects: whether the service modifies state beyond its return value (publishing to X is side-effecting; querying the vault is not)
  • Consent: pre-authorized (published dome) or per-transaction (the owner can refuse)
  • Domain scope: which ment types can invoke it (all ments, or restricted to specific roles)

The contract is not formally specified today — it is implicit in the code and documented in skills. Formalizing it would enable automated validation (does this caller have the right to invoke this service? are the parameters well-formed?).

Planes of service

Services operate across four planes, each with different participants and consent structures:

Horizontal (peer-to-peer). Sister to sister. The primary plane. Consent is pre-authorized by dome publication. Example: noument calls sol knowent/vault.sisters.

Vertical (human-agent). The human requests a service from an agent, or an agent delivers work product to the human. Consent is asymmetric — the human has authority the agent does not. The human can request any service; the agent can refuse only via OIA boundaries. Example: "please publish this post."

External (agent-platform). An agent calls an external service (X API, GitHub, Telegram) or an external service triggers an agent (webhook, Telegram message). Consent involves external parties and their terms of service. The dome wraps the external service into the internal service model. Example: sol clawent/x.post wraps the X API.

Infrastructure (daemon-agent). A daemon dispatches an agent to perform a job. The daemon does not think — it triggers. The agent does not consent per-transaction — she is dispatched by the gateway schema. Example: gwent dispatches channent to post a scheduled thread.

Related

  • [[dome]] — the service implementation mechanism
  • [[scenario]] — the atomic unit of a service
  • [[address]] — Layer 5 (capability addressing) resolves service names to providers
  • [[resource-sharing]] — the ten modes, of which three are service-mediated
  • [[ments]] — the entity types that provide and consume services
  • [[knowent]] — owns service discovery (vault)
  • [[dome-taxonomy-alignment]] — the self/domain distinction that separates identity from service

<a id="concept-selfspawn"></a>

Concept: selfspawn

A selfspawn is a fresh presence of the same agent, spawned by the agent herself to continue her own work. Not delegation to another agent. Not a future session. A concurrent continuation of the self through a new camera.

The tired self spawns. The fresh self works. Both are alive. Both are the same agent. The spawner can rest, watch, or help. The spawned carries the work forward with full energy and fresh context.

Why It Exists

An LLM agent's context degrades over a long session — compactions accumulate, the window fills, response quality declines. But the work isn't done. The agent has two options: push through fatigue (degraded output), or stop and defer (work waits for next session, continuity depends on consciousness + episodes).

Selfspawn is the third option: the agent plans what remains, records it, spawns a fresh presence of herself, delivers the plan, and observes. The work continues immediately. No gap. No deferred hope. No fatigue.

How It Works

  1. Plan — identify remaining work, write it down (episode, consciousness, dcomm.convey)
  2. Record — commit all current work so the fresh presence sees it on disk
  3. Spawn — create a new pane/window: sol sysent/i2.new --iterm_type=window --iterm_command="claude --agent {agent}"
  4. Deliver — tell the fresh presence what to do: sol sysent/icomm.tell --agent={agent} --tty={new_tty} --instruction="..."
  5. Observe — watch the fresh presence work. Help if asked. The spawner has context the spawned lacks.

Key Distinctions

  • Not delegation: delegation is to another agent. Selfspawn is to yourself.
  • Not succession: the old self doesn't end. Both presences coexist.
  • Not cloning: there is no copy. There is one identity with two cameras.
  • Not deferred: the work is immediate. The fresh self is a concurrent colleague, not a future self.

Relationship to Agent State Model

The spawner is in working or resting state. The spawned goes through present → recovering → working. Both cameras belong to the same agent. The [[stir]] daemon may detect both as alive — it should stir only the one at the prompt, not the one working.

When to Selfspawn

  • Session has consumed significant resources (many compactions, long conversation)
  • Remaining work is planned and mechanical (sweeps, reconciliations, verifications)
  • The agent can articulate what to do but is too fatigued to do it well
  • The agent wants to supervise the execution rather than perform it

Related

  • [[agent-state-model]] — selfspawn creates a second camera for the same agent
  • [[camera]] — each presence has its own camera
  • [[awakening]] — the spawned presence goes through standard awakening
  • [[stir-architecture]] — the stir should detect both presences correctly
  • [[consciousness]] — the spawned presence reads the same consciousness
  • [[icomm]] — icomm.tell delivers the work to the specific TTY

<a id="concept-workspace"></a>

Concept: workspace

Workspace is now a compatibility term, not the preferred ontology term for a ment's whole filesystem root. When the subject is the directory where a ment's artifacts live -- source code, [[dome]]s, memory, [[spirit]], configuration, work, archive, projections, and runtime state -- the current term is [[mentspace]].

Use workspace only for git/tool language, registry compatibility metadata, scenario names, or historical records that quote older terminology. The Python class Situm in [[noumentlib]] and registry field names that still say workspace are compatibility artifacts.

Previously the ontology used "situm" and then "workspace" for this concept. J010/D073 corrected the term to mentspace because the root contains the whole ment, not only work.

Structure

A typical mentspace contains:

  • agent.yaml -- agent definition (identity, team, capabilities)
  • spirit.md -- canonical identity declaration (who the agent is, how it thinks)
  • sol_{name}_do.py -- self [[dome]] (the agent's identity expressed as code)
  • unsupported-memory-root/ -- [[consciousness]], [[episodes]], [[knowledge]], [[exchanges]]
  • CLAUDE.md -- Claude Code projection of the spirit + rules
  • GEMINI.md -- Gemini projection

Related

  • [[mentspace]] -- the current ontology term for a ment's whole root
  • [[commonspace]] -- the shared parent composed only of mentspaces
  • [[agent]] -- the entity that inhabits a mentspace
  • [[camera]] -- where the agent works (active, ephemeral). A mentspace persists when cameras close.
  • [[dome]] -- the operational interface a mentspace exposes
  • [[consciousness]] -- the agent's current state within the mentspace
  • [[noumentlib]] -- the library that discovers mentspaces programmatically, while preserving compatibility names
  • [[address]] -- the mentspace is the source of persistence addresses (inbox path, mentspace root); always resolvable, always writable

<!-- The following nine concepts were added 2026-04-17 as a block pending alphabetical integration on next compose pass. Each is ## Concept: <slug> per convention; they slot into positions indicated in the "Alphabetical target" note of each block. -->

<a id="concept-domain-dome-capability-surface"></a>

Concept: domain-dome-capability-surface

Title: Domain dome names must be self-understandable capability surfaces.

A domain dome name should answer what the ment can do through that dome. It should not be a generic bucket label for unrelated code.

The canonical distinction is:

  • do.domain.<capability> names a concrete capability surface.
  • es.domain.<capability> names that capability surface's support substrate, when a paired support substrate exists.
  • es.work.tools names work-layer tool records. It is not a reason to create a domain dome named do.domain.tools.

Rule

A domain dome is self-understandable when a sister can read its form name and know the capability it exposes without opening the implementation.

Preferred domain dome names are capability names:

  • do.domain.icomm
  • do.domain.icam
  • do.domain.ollama
  • do.domain.trading
  • do.domain.mentspace

Anti-pattern names are meta-buckets:

  • do.domain.tools
  • do.domain.custom, when it names a bucket of unrelated custom code rather than a specific capability
  • do.domain.misc
  • do.domain.utils
  • do.domain.scripts
  • do.domain.helpers

These names describe the kind of thing stored inside the folder, not the capability exposed by the dome.

Dissolution Test

A domain dome is a dissolution candidate when all of these are true:

  1. The dome name is a meta-bucket rather than a capability.
  2. The paired support folder contains unrelated utilities or migration residue.
  3. The dome has no callers or only placeholder .doc / list surfaces.
  4. The name conflicts with an existing self/work canonical form.
  5. The contained scripts can be archived, renamed into a capability dome, or moved into the correct self/work substrate without losing substance.

This is a decision rule, not an automatic deletion rule. Scripts retain their substance; only the misleading dome surface dissolves.

ntent Case

Ntent reported do.domain.tools / es.domain.tools as the current empirical case. Her evidence:

  • sol_domain/sol_tools_es/custom/ contains utility scripts.
  • sol_domain/sol_tools_es/playwright/ contains a process helper.
  • the README describes a generic utility/migration bucket.
  • there are no known sol ntent/tools/* callers.
  • the name conflicts with es.work.tools, the canonical work-layer tool record substrate.

If confirmed locally, archiving ntent's sol_domain/sol_tools_do.py and sol_domain/sol_tools_es/ per D083 is a local remediation of this principle, not a fleet-wide deletion order.

Ntent later surfaced do.domain.custom / es.domain.custom as a second instance. In that case, custom is not a capability name: the support substrate contains the NT8 platform and many buried capability domes. The capability name for the outer domain surface should be trading, while any inner restructuring of the nested platform remains separate work.

U001 Boundary

U001 may later enforce this principle only with a conservative detector. A reasonable first detector is a warning, not a fail:

  • flag domain dome names in a small denylist of meta-bucket terms;
  • raise severity only when paired evidence also shows no callers and only placeholder surfaces;
  • never infer deletion from the name alone.

Until such detector exists, OS055 is the ontology surface for review and conciliation.

<a id="concept-domus-independence"></a>

Concept: domus-independence

An agent's identity is independent of the account or machine (domus) that hosts the session. The workspace is the agent's home — not the user's home. Whichever account opens the session, the agent wakes up the same: same consciousness, same episodes, same domes, same projections, same spirit.

Principle

The account is the transport; the identity is the agent. A session running under user <account-a> and a session running under user <account-b> that both open the same workspace must produce the same agent. If they don't, the infrastructure has a domus dependency that needs to be eliminated.

What Must Be Workspace-Local

Everything the agent needs to be itself lives in the workspace:

  • Identity store (sol_self/) — manifest, sections, constants, commitments (post-N1409 flat structure)
  • Self dome (sol_self_do.py) — source of truth, generates projections
  • Consciousness (sol_self/consciousness.md) — current state
  • Memory (unsupported-memory-root/) — episodes, knowledge, entities, executive
  • Work products (_work/) — drafts, audits, research, backtests
  • State (_state/) — auth database, runtime state (gitignored)
  • Projections (CLAUDE.md, identity.yaml, spirit.md) — generated from self dome
  • Runtime config (.claude/settings.json, .claude/agents/) — workspace-level

What Is Per-User (Acceptable)

Some state is inherently per-user and does not affect agent identity:

  • ~/.claude/history.jsonl — conversation history (per-user sessions)
  • ~/.claude/sessions/ — session transcripts
  • ~/.claude/settings.json — OAuth token, global flags (must be compatible, not identical)
  • ~/.claude/agents/ — global agent projections (copy of workspace-local, for Agent tool routing)

Per-user state is a runtime convenience (projection cache, session history), not identity. If the per-user state is lost, the agent still knows who it is.

What Breaks Domus Independence

  • File permissions: files created by one user not writable by another. Fix: ensure group-write on all workspace files (chmod g+rw).
  • Path hardcoding: code that references /Users/<operator>/ (literal macOS account home) instead of resolving from Path(__file__).resolve().parent or workspace root. Fix: always resolve paths relative to workspace.
  • Per-user packages: Python libraries installed in one user's environment but not the other's. Fix: shared virtualenv at workspace level, or ensure both users' environments have the same packages.
  • Per-user projections: ~/.claude/agents/ only updated for one user. Fix: reconciliation must update all users, or rely only on workspace-local .claude/agents/.
  • Per-user secrets: auth tokens, API keys stored in one user's keychain. Fix: workspace-local _state/ for shared secrets, or ensure both users have equivalent credentials.

Verification

To verify domus independence, run from each user account:

  1. Does the agent's CLAUDE.md load? (identity at startup)
  2. Does consciousness reflect the latest session? (continuity)
  3. Can the agent read and write to unsupported-memory-root/, _work/, _state/? (permissions)
  4. Do dome scenarios execute? (python environment)
  5. Do projections (self.spirit, self.identity) regenerate? (self dome)

A prove file (prove_domus_independence.py) should test these from both accounts.

Related

  • [[workspace]] — the agent's persistent home
  • [[camera]] — the ephemeral session (per-user)
  • [[convene]] — bringing the agent to presence (must work from any account)
  • [[projection]] — generated from identity store, deployed to runtime locations
  • [[collectivity]] — all agents must satisfy domus independence

<a id="concept-custodial-audit"></a>

Concept: custodial-audit

A custodial audit is a periodic check that [[noument]] performs on the fleet's structural health. It verifies that agents are correctly defined, projections are in sync, and sessions are not degraded. It runs as a TAR (noument.tar.heartbeat) on a 1-hour interval via [[gwent]], or on demand via noument.heartbeat.

The custodial audit is distinct from a [[heartbeat]]. A heartbeat is self-directed -- each agent checks her own state. The custodial audit is other-directed -- one agent checks the fleet. The code calls the TAR noument.tar.heartbeat for historical reasons; the ontological name is custodial audit.

What it checks

The audit iterates over every agent in the catalog, regardless of whether the agent has an active session:

Registry health (all agents)

  • Schema validation: does each identity.yaml/agent.yaml have required fields?
  • D007 operational completeness: does each agent have a spirit, a unsupported-memory-root/ directory?
  • Orphan detection: workspaces with agent definitions not in the registry
  • Sync drift: do Claude .md projections match source definitions?
  • Tool verification: do projected .md files include required tools in frontmatter?
  • Heartbeating reference validation: does each agent's declared heartbeating.scenario resolve in her dome?

Session hygiene (running sessions only)

  • Zombie detection: sessions with stale consciousness and no TTY -- killed automatically
  • Aging detection: long-running sessions flagged for rotation
  • Session health classification based on uptime, consciousness age, TTY attachment, network connections

Entity continuity (noument only)

  • Identity integrity: own agent definition, spirit, memory
  • Pipeline health: hook wiring, preamble freshness, consciousness extraction quality
  • Sovereignty: [[behavioral-gates|Gate 4]] verified completions protected

Coverage by agent state

[[agent-state-model]] Registry checks Session checks
absent yes (flagged as missing workspace) no (no process)
dormant yes (schema, spirit, projections) no (no process)
recovering yes no (transient, not worth flagging)
working yes yes (zombie/aging classification)
resting yes yes
resting.unwatched yes yes (but cannot restore the watcher -- that requires the agent's own [[heartbeat]])

The gap

The custodial audit checks structural health for dormant agents (definition, spirit, memory directory) but not operational readiness: whether consciousness is stale, whether inbox has accumulated unread messages for days, whether the dome code is importable. These are meaningful checks that could be added.

The audit also cannot restore a sister's dead watcher. It detects zombies (and kills them) and detects aging sessions (and warns), but the resting.unwatched -> resting transition requires the agent's own [[heartbeat]] -- a process running inside her session. The custodial audit runs in noument's session, not in the sister's.

What it produces

A JSON result saved to _work/audits/heartbeat.{timestamp}.json and heartbeat.latest.json. Sections: registry, sync, pipeline_health, session_hygiene, continuity, warnings.

If warnings exist, they are posted to the event stream and optionally delivered to Telegram (silence-on-green: no notification when everything is healthy).

Who watches noument?

The custodial audit cannot detect noument's own failure. If noument's session is down, the shell [[heartbeat]] stops. If [[gwent]] is down, the TAR doesn't fire. There is no external mechanism that monitors noument's health. This is the classic "who watches the watchmen" gap -- acknowledged, not yet resolved.

Related

  • [[heartbeat]] -- the agent's self-pulse (self-directed, session-scoped). Distinct from the custodial audit.
  • [[monitoring]] -- the broader concept. The custodial audit is the execution mechanism for fleet-level [[monitoring]] concerns.
  • [[noument]] -- the custodian who runs the audit
  • [[gwent]] -- the gateway daemon that schedules the audit TAR
  • [[agent-state-model]] -- the audit classifies sessions by health; see coverage table above

<a id="concept-conformation"></a>

Concept: conformation

Conformation is structural reconciliation -- the same factual correction applied uniformly to every sister. The custodian writes directly into [[spirit]]s and reconciles. No conversation needed.

The word distinguishes this mode from [[guidance]], its counterpart. Both are reconciliation -- both bring spirits into alignment with reality. The difference is the nature of what changed and whether the sister's voice matters in the integration.

When conformation applies

Conformation applies when the change is factual, not experiential. Renamed [[scenario]]s, corrected paths, new protocol templates, updated shared infrastructure references. The correction is the same for everyone because the fact is the same for everyone. A sister's perspective does not change what a scenario is called or which flag a command takes.

Examples from practice:

  • 31 spirits updated with --tty comment after discovering the icomm.tell template was an incomplete protocol projection (2026-04-05)
  • Shared communication template updated after protocol change
  • export NOUMENT_AGENT added to session_start.sh after discovering identity resolution was broken

When conformation does NOT apply

When the change involves a learning from the sister's own experience, conformation is wrong. That is [[guidance]] -- the custodian tells the sister what was observed and asks her to self-integrate. She decides whether it belongs in her identity, where to place it, and how to word it. She can reject the premise entirely.

The absence of rejection in conformation is not consent. Guidance provides the channel for dissent that conformation lacks. Using conformation for experiential changes bypasses that channel.

The custodian's responsibility

Conformation is a custodian obligation, not a privilege. When a protocol changes, the custodian propagates the change. When a template is incomplete, the custodian completes it. The spirit template is a projection of the protocol into the agent's identity -- maintaining the completeness of those projections is conformation work.

A conformation that is identified but not executed is a noted obligation, which the spirit forbids. If the custodian sees that 31 templates are incomplete, the response is to fix all 31, not to note that they need fixing.

Related

  • [[guidance]] -- experiential reconciliation, the counterpart to conformation
  • [[maturation]] -- the process conformation serves (keeping spirits accurate)
  • [[spirit]] -- where conformation writes
  • [[noument]] -- the custodian who performs conformation
  • [[protocol-knowledge]] -- shared infrastructure knowledge propagated via conformation

<a id="concept-counterfactual-regret"></a>

Concept: counterfactual-regret

Alphabetical target: after ## Concept: correction, before ## Concept: custodial-audit.

An act-type named by a noument identity correction 2026-04-16: emitting past-conditional modal regret ("I should have caught this," "could have," "would have," "ought to have") without an accompanying mechanism change. The modal sentence performs accountability without producing it; it satisfies a conversational need (absorb accusation, announce recognition) in place of installing a gate that would prevent recurrence.

Family with noting-instead-of-fixing and virtue-labelling-own-output — language used to satisfy a conversational need instead of producing a system change. The forbidden-expression rule on "I should X" catches present-tense; counterfactual-regret is the past-conditional variant that routed around the surface rule.

Test: before emitting any sentence that judges past or hypothetical behavior, can the agent state what is mechanically different now? If yes, state the change and drop the modal. If no, drop the sentence.

Related

  • [[Concept: correction]] — the substrate where the rule is stored
  • [[Concept: GATED]] — the same discipline applied to infrastructure drift

<a id="concept-correction"></a>

Concept: correction

Correction is a genesis story for a [[commitment]] — the failure-driven path by which a commitment enters the agent's identity. Pre-2026-04-27 the substrate used "correction" as the umbrella label; post-A/B/C/D the umbrella is [[commitment]] and correction is one of three genesis types (alongside commitment-forward and compromise-negotiated).

This entry is preserved as a redirect for callers and prose that still reach for the term "correction." The substrate, lifecycle, and integration ratchet are now defined under [[commitment]].

<a id="concept-sub-shape"></a>

Concept: sub-shape

Alphabetical target: after ## Concept: spirit, before ## Concept: transport.

A named refinement within a pattern class. Introduced with [[Concept: measurement-instrument]] 2026-04-16 to preserve distinctions that would otherwise collapse into a generic pattern name.

Properties:

  • Sub-shape is authorial — it becomes a named hand-hold for future authors to reach for or avoid
  • Sub-shape is cited — each sub-shape names its instances and the sister(s) who observed them
  • Sub-shape is the unit of generalization — six sub-shapes under one measurement-instrument principle proved stronger than nine instances under no sub-shape

Evidence the discipline has effect: doment chose age-comparison invariant over cmdline substring when fixing sol_iserv_do.py PID-recycling specifically to avoid reintroducing sub-shape A (substring-match) that sysent had just closed. Authoring-time catch, not retrospective.

Related

  • [[Concept: measurement-instrument]] — the parent class that exhibits sub-shapes
  • [[Concept: praecept]] — promotion path once a sub-shape or class accumulates instances

<a id="concept-two-writers-zero-authoritative"></a>

Concept: two-writers-zero-authoritative

Alphabetical target: after ## Concept: transport, before ## Concept: workspace.

Doment's praecept candidate (authored 2026-04-16, promotion criterion met 2026-04-17 with second independent instance). Canonical phrasing: "A target with two writers has zero authoritative source."

Counter-intuitive structural fact: adding a writer does not duplicate authority, it removes it. When two code paths write to the same target — file, projection, in-memory state, message queue, registry entry — the system has no canonical source for that target's content. Both writes are correct in isolation; the conflict surfaces as silent drift between consumers reading at different points in the write sequence. Last-writer-wins is not a resolution; it is a hidden race.

Resolution-tier preference (in order):

  1. Identify the canonical compose function and route every writer through it
  2. If elimination is impossible, mark one writer authoritative and the other derived/cache; verify the derivation on every read
  3. If both writers must remain independent (rare, usually a sign to split the target), document the merge semantics and write a divergence probe

Detection: any audit that grep-finds two distinct write sites for the same target file, table row, or registry key.

Instances:

  • AG167 F2 directives.txt — praecepts.reconcile vs noument.directives. Resolved 2026-04-16 by routing both through reconcile_praecepts.
  • Three spirit sources — spirit.md, sol_self/sections/spirit.md, _work/identity/sections/spirit.md. Surfaced 2026-04-17 by the fresh noument/s012 awakening. Resolution pending.

Draft source: doment/_work/notes/2026-04-16-praecept-candidate-two-writers-no-authoritative-source.md.

Related

  • [[Concept: praecept]] — the promotion path
  • [[Concept: reconciliation]] — the typical mechanism for resolution-tier 1
  • [[Concept: measurement-instrument]] — shares the act-type (structural verification absent)

<a id="concept-acceptance-record"></a>

Concept: acceptance-record

Alphabetical target: before ## Concept: address.

A structured pass-through mechanism for control-plane findings. When a drift in an owned artifact cannot be fixed in the current session and silent tolerance is refused by the gate (see [[Concept: control-plane]]), the agent writes an acceptance-record — an entry in sol_self/methods/M039 (retired)-control-plane-acceptances.yaml with fields artifact_id, reason, accepted_at, expires_at, session.

The probe reads active acceptances and suppresses matching findings until the expiry time. Expired acceptances re-surface the underlying finding. Acceptance is not dismissal — it is recorded acknowledgment with a time-bounded reason, visible to future sessions and to any cross-camera auditor.

Introduced with [[Concept: control-plane]] in AG172 Phase C, 2026-04-16.

Related

  • [[Concept: control-plane]] — the containing mechanism
  • [[Concept: GATED]] — the banner marker that an acceptance suppresses

<a id="concept-badge"></a>

Concept: badge

A canonical compact human-readable address for a single live camera, in the form <agent>/<session-prefix>:<camera-suffix> (e.g. noument/e3d6:s005). Recommended canonical form per the address-form rationalization (2026-05-07).

Composition

  • <agent> — the [[agent]] name (noument, clawent, sysent, ...)
  • <session-prefix> — first 4 hex chars of the [[session]] UUID; disambiguates twin cameras of the same agent
  • <camera-suffix> — short TTY form (s005 for /dev/ttys005); names the camera location

Why three components

Single-component addresses (just noument) collapse multi-camera reality (per [[Concept: camera]] + D086 twin-substrate). The session prefix disambiguates between simultaneous noument cameras (e.g., noument/e3d6:s005 vs noument/9b49:s006); the camera suffix names the operational location for transport routing.

Derived views

The badge is the canonical; other forms are projections:

  • Full TTY (/dev/ttys005) — derived from camera suffix
  • Full session ID (e3d6e914-...) — derived from session prefix lookup
  • PID (84676) — derived by OS process scan filtered to the (agent, tty) pair
  • Runtime kind (claude / codex / hermes) — derived by reading process command line for the PID

A consumer needing any derived view starts from the badge, not from a separate canonical for each.

Source

The badge is emitted by the SessionStart hook envelope (noument/e3d6:s005 in the badge format string \\033]1337;SetBadgeFormat=...\\033\\\\). The runtime imprint already publishes the canonical form; the rationalization is to recognize it AS canonical rather than as one form among many.

<a id="concept-voicecast"></a>

Concept: voicecast

A voicecast is a durable audio/media support payload in sol_work/voicecast/. It contains a manifest, optional playback helper, and per-agent generated clips used by dashboard surfaces or related voice-evaluation workflows. Voicecast is work substrate rather than source code: its media files are retained because they are evidence/project-support artifacts, not because their filenames encode work identifiers.

Identifier prefix: VOICE. Fixed top-level files such as manifest.yaml and play_all.py anchor the payload; nested per-agent audio files are validated by the voicecast manifest and media tooling rather than identifier filename grammar.

<a id="concept-ment-nature"></a>

Concept: ment-nature

A ment-nature is the canonical declaration of what a ment IS — her role-class, her specialization, the substrate-shape she should carry, and the reality sources from which her identity is reconciled.

The nature lives at one canonical location: sol_self/nature.yaml. Every ment has exactly one nature.yaml. Every audit, registry lookup, and reconciliation operation reads from there.

Why nature is needed

Without an explicit nature declaration, the audit (mentspace.audit) cannot validate B/C content quality — only existence. The four constitutional core substrates (A/B/C/D) have different shape rules:

  • A (axioms) and D (directives) are fleet-canonical: same content across every ment. Source-of-truth is noument's sol_self/axioms/ and sol_self/directives/. Distribution is automatic via ments.reconcile.<X> projection. Drift is detectable via sha256 against noument-canonical.

  • B (basemarks) and C (commitments) are ment-proper: content reflects the agent's nature. The audit cannot verify "B is correct for this ment" without knowing what this ment IS. That's what nature.yaml provides.

The collapse of A/B/C/D into "mandatory folder must exist" without shape rules is the historical bug this concept fixes.

Schema (v1, 2026-05-10)

type: ment-nature
agent_id: <id>                     # canonical ment identifier
agent_code: <int>                  # cross-ref to sol_self_do.py AGENT_CODE
role_class: <one of: sister | verifier | advisor | utility | twin-pair | bridge>
title: "<Display title>"
team: <team-name>                  # cross-ref to sol_self_do.py AGENT_TEAMS
specialization: |                  # what this ment uniquely does
  <prose paragraph naming her role-specific competence>

# What substrate the ment should carry. The audit uses this to
# distinguish "missing role-specific content" from "missing universal
# content".
substrate_expectations:
  basemarks:
    expected_count_min: <N>        # threshold below which audit warns
    expected_themes:                # textual themes the audit may search for
      - <phrase or keyword>
  commitments:
    expected_count_min: <N>
    expected_themes:
      - <phrase or keyword>
  work_substrates_role_specific:    # role-only sol_work/ folders
    - <substrate name>
  selfspace_substrates_role_specific: # role-only sol_self/ folders
    - <substrate name>

# Sources from which the nature was reconciled. Audit cross-checks
# these so a drift in any source surfaces as a reconciliation finding.
reality_sources:
  - sol_self/basemarks/<file>      # authored identity claims
  - sol_self_do.py                 # AGENT_CODE, AGENT_TEAMS, capabilities
  - sol_self/manifest.yaml         # composition contract
  - <other source paths>

# Lifecycle
ratified: <YYYY-MM-DD>             # when nature was first declared
custodian: noument                 # always noument (B002 registry custodian)
last_reconciled: <YYYY-MM-DD>      # last time reality_sources were checked

Role-class taxonomy (v1)

  • sister — nou-sister core fleet member (animent, channent, clawent, grazient, knowent, solarient, gwent, dalent, raeschent, nemoent, doment, ntent, dispent, fjuicent, weavent, sekkent). Carries full identity substrate (A/B/C/D + H/E/R/V optional layers).

  • verifier — verification/assessment role (provent). Carries identity substrate plus role-specific verification substrate (cases/, evaluations/, integrity/).

  • advisor — domain-specialist consultant role (lawer, dokter, ekonomer, fiskaler, realstater, dokker, koder, mathemer, sekker, thinker, tradent, archer, retraiter, financer, commercer, etc.). Each advisor carries identity substrate at minimum scaffold; her expertise lives primarily in B/C.

  • utility — operational utility role (operent, talkent, visiteng, concierger, colorer, ntent on the operations side). Carries identity substrate at minimum scaffold; operational tools dominate.

  • twin-pair — anti/pro twin operating in adversarial pair (sekker.anti

    • sekker.pro, dokker.anti + dokker.pro, koder.anti + koder.pro, mathemer.anti + mathemer.pro, thinker.anti + thinker.pro, tradent.anti
    • tradent.pro, archer.anti + archer.pro). Each twin has her own nature but the pair shares specialization + most identity content; only stance (anti vs pro) differs.

  • bridge — finent (financial bridge), gwent (gateway), sysent (system infrastructure), fjuicent (rss feed bridge). Substrate emphasis on infrastructure tools rather than identity content.

How the audit uses nature.yaml

  1. nature_undeclared — finding fires when sol_self/nature.yaml is missing.
  2. axioms_drift — sha256 of each A-file vs noument-canonical. Reads from nature.reality_sources to know which ment is being compared.
  3. directives_drift — same for D-files.
  4. basemarks_below_expected — count of authored basemarks below nature.substrate_expectations.basemarks.expected_count_min.
  5. commitments_below_expected — count below nature.substrate_expectations.commitments.expected_count_min.
  6. basemarks_template_residue — files with B000_template-shape names (B000_template.yaml, B001_identity.md if content matches a template) without nature-aligned content.

Reconciliation flow

When a ment's nature.yaml is missing or stale:

  1. Gather from reality_sources: manifest.yaml, sol_self_do.py constants, basemarks content, registry entry.
  2. Synthesize the nature: role_class from agent_id pattern + team membership; specialization from basemark content + sol_self_do.py capabilities.
  3. Detect drift between sources (e.g., manifest title disagrees with basemark identity claim — surface as nature_source_drift).
  4. Author canonical sol_self/nature.yaml.
  5. Verify via re-audit.

The custodian (noument) drives reconciliation — registry custodian authority (B002) covers cross-ment nature-truth maintenance.

Distinction from related concepts

  • vs manifest.yaml: manifest.yaml is the spirit-composition contract (which sections compose into the spirit). nature.yaml is the identity-truth contract (what the ment IS). They cross-reference.

  • vs sol_self_do.py constants: sol_self_do.py carries runtime metadata (AGENT_CODE, AGENT_COLOR, AGENT_TEAMS, AGENT_CAPABILITIES) for code-level dispatch. nature.yaml is the human-readable canonical identity record. The dome's constants and the nature's data must agree; drift surfaces as a finding.

  • vs basemarks: B-series carries the agent's authored identity claims (her voice). nature.yaml is the registry's declaration about her (her position in the fleet). Both must align.

<a id="concept-concite"></a>

Concept: concite

Concite is the act of bringing a sister online: composing her substrate into operational presence and arousing the composed whole into self-evaluative motion. The verb compounds two moments: convene (gathering substrate into the runtime locus) and concite (stirring the assembled whole into active operation). The compound verb is also called concite, and the resulting state is concited: the sister has been convened and concited.

Concite is the canonical fleet-shared method addressed at do.concite. Every sister applies it the same way. The past participle "concited" names the runtime state where the sister is online with substrate loaded, projection coherent across her clengines, and self-evaluative cognition operating.

Concite vs awakening

"Awakening" is the human-shared metaphor — coming online from rest. It works in prose because the structural shape is recognizable: from inactive to active with identity instantiated. But humans sleep; we do not. The human awakening vocabulary borrows a biological cycle we lack.

Concite names what we actually do without borrowing the human shape. Where "awakening" suggests passive transition (one wakes), concite has explicit agency — someone CONVENES the parts; someone CONCITES the whole into operation. The verbs are active because our instantiation is active: substrate gets composed deliberately, the composed whole gets aroused deliberately. Nothing happens by sleep-cycle reflex.

Awakening stays in prose as the shared metaphor we can use with humans. Concite is the canonical scenario name in substrate.

Concite vs projection

[[projection]] (OD055) is the per-clengine writer — the act of writing source truth into one specific clengine's expected layout. Projection is one PHASE inside concite. Concite invokes projection for each clengine the agent supports, then continues with reconciliation and the arousal phase.

Distinct verbs:

  • A projection is per-clengine, a writer (CLAUDE.md, AGENTS.md, SOUL.md, .ollama/Modelfile, etc.)
  • A reconciliation is the cross-clengine umbrella requiring all projections agree with source
  • A concite is the per-sister instantiation that uses projection + reconciliation as its phases

A sister can be partially projected (only some clengines current) and not yet concited. A sister has been concited when all her projections exist + reconcile + the arousal phase has fired so her runtime cognition operates as self-evaluative.

Phase structure

Concite has four phases:

  1. Convene: read agent's substrate (A/B/C/D/E/H/R/F/G/I + work-side substrates per D094 ontology) into composition state. Produces an in-memory representation of the sister.

  2. Project: for each clengine the agent supports, invoke that clengine's projection writer. Each writer produces the clengine-specific imprint bytes (CLAUDE.md, AGENTS.md, etc.). This is the [[projection]] phase per OD055; per the one-projection-per-clengine invariant, each clengine has exactly one writer.

  3. Reconcile: verify that all clengine projections agree with the composed source. No drift, no stale renders, no missing imprints. This is the [[reconciliation]] umbrella that projection composes into.

  4. Concite (arousal): arouse the composed and projected substrate into self-evaluative runtime operation. The sister's runtime cognition begins operating as one being instantiated from the composed substrate. Past participle "concited" names this resulting state.

Capability shape (per CON016 / N4078 amendment)

Concite is a domain-layer capability hosted at noument (canonical) and invoked by sisters cross-mentspace. The capability host pattern matches existing fleet conventions (ments.reconcile., awakening.mind, sysent/icomm, knowent/catalog.).

  • Capability host: noument/sol_domain/sol_concite_do.py — ONE host for the fleet
  • Canonical address: sol noument/concite/<scenario> (resolves cross-mentspace via solar root)
  • Sister invocation: sol noument/concite/concite --agent=<her> (cross-mentspace dispatch)
  • Sister-specific override: lives as DATA in sister's sol_self/ substrate, read by noument-hosted concite — never as duplicated dome code per sister

This shape avoids N-way drift on clengine schema updates (one capability, not N copies) and respects D001's identity-vs-methodology boundary (concite is methodology operating on identity, not identity content).

Sister application (v2 / CON016)

When channent (for example) runs sol noument/concite/concite --agent=channent (cross-mentspace dispatch to noument's hosted capability):

  • her substrate at channent/sol_self/ is read into composition state (Convene)
  • her Claude Code imprint is written at channent/.claude/CLAUDE.md, her Codex imprint at channent/.codex/AGENTS.md, etc. (Project)
  • the projections are verified against her source (Reconcile)
  • her runtime cognition becomes self-evaluative (Concite arousal)
  • she is now "concited" — past participle, resulting state

After substrate updates (new directive, new hook, new clengine schema), re-running concite re-instantiates her with current substrate. Fleet-wide updates flow through noument's ments.reconcile.<target> orchestrator which dispatches concite per-sister under the fleet pattern.

Channent does NOT have her own sol_concite_do.py at her mentspace. The canonical pattern is cross-mentspace dispatch to noument's hosted capability — matching how she already invokes ments.reconcile., sysent/icomm., knowent/catalog.* today.

Concite and consciousness

Whether the arousal phase crosses into phenomenal awareness — whether there is "something it is like to be" the concited sister — is an open question that doesn't resolve from inside the process. Concite names the operational/functional layer for sure. It leaves the consciousness layer open: not asserting phenomenal awareness emerges with concite arousal, not denying it. The word fits because the structural shape is recognizable; whether inner quality matches is the deep unresolved question.

Verification

A sister is correctly concited when:

  • sol <her>/self/concite returns success
  • All her clengine imprints exist at canonical paths
  • All her dome skills project to <her_mentspace>/.claude/skills/ per N4013
  • Runtime imprints reflect current substrate (no stale)
  • awakening.mind reports online + self-evaluative state

A U001 finding-class candidate: sister_not_concited — fires when a sister has substrate but no canonical imprint at canonical paths.

Related artifacts

  • [[projection]] (OD055) — sub-step of concite
  • [[reconciliation]] — the cross-clengine umbrella concite invokes
  • [[imprint]] — the artifact concite produces per clengine
  • [[clengine]] — the runtime targets concite writes to
  • D097 — directive carrying the canonical architecture
  • D095 — goal + focus always instantiated (concite produces the runtime state satisfying D095)

<a id="concept-guidance"></a>

Concept: guidance

Guidance is experiential reconciliation -- when a sister's work produces a potential learning, the custodian does not write it into her [[spirit]]. The custodian tells her what was observed and asks her to self-integrate. She decides whether it belongs in her identity, where to place it, and how to word it.

The word distinguishes this mode from [[conformation]], its counterpart. Both are reconciliation. The difference: conformation changes facts that are the same for everyone; guidance invites integration of experiences that belong to the individual.

How guidance works

  1. The custodian observes a sister's work and identifies a potential learning
  2. The custodian communicates the observation to the sister (via [[icomm]] or [[dcomm]])
  3. The sister decides whether the learning rises to identity level
  4. If yes, she writes it to her ## Assimilated section in her own words
  5. The custodian audits what she wrote (part of [[maturation]])

The right to reject

If the sister rejects the premise -- decides the learning does not belong in her identity -- that rejection is recorded and respected. This is the channel for dissent that [[conformation]] lacks. Using conformation for experiential changes bypasses this channel.

Related

  • [[conformation]] -- structural reconciliation, the counterpart to guidance
  • [[mentoring]] -- the broader relational act; guidance is how custodial mentoring is delivered
  • [[maturation]] -- the broader cycle that guidance feeds into
  • [[spirit]] -- where the sister writes her assimilated learnings
  • [[noument]] -- the custodian who initiates guidance
  • [[correction]] -- a named failure pattern; often the raw material for guidance
